📚 本期文献进展 / Literature Brief

  • 本期SARS-CoV-2研究集中呈现以下进展。
  • 共有 135 名参与者参与,其中超过 90% 的人感染了免疫逃避的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变种。没有报告与 治疗 相关的不良事件或严重不良事件。探索性分析显示,200 mg SNS812 组达到 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 抗原阴性的中位时间较短(2.9 天 vs 3.6 天;p = 0.007),病毒载量降低率更快(-0.755 vs -0.652;p = 0.040)。
  • 关于 新型冠状病毒感染 疫苗 在 Omicron 变异占优势期间有效性的可靠证据对于为全球 疫苗接种 策略提供信息至关重要。我们受世界卫生组织 (WHO) 委托进行的活体系统评价旨在对比较观察性研究进行持续综合和严格评估,评估 疫苗 对 Omicron 变体引起的 新型冠状病毒感染/严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的有效性。截至 2022 年 12 月 31 日,每周检索七个数据库,寻找 WHO 批准的 疫苗 平台的证据,包括 mRNA 疫苗(BNT162b2、mRNA-1273)、腺病毒 媒介 疫苗(ChAdOx1-S、SII-ChAdOx1 nCoV-19、 Ad26.COV2·S、Gam-COVID-Vac) 和灭活病毒 疫苗 (CoronaVac、BBV-152)。我们使用干预措施非随机研究中的偏倚风险 (ROBINS-I) 工具来评估 疫苗 有效性估计的有效性。该综述涵盖了超过 5300 万人的 79 项研究,贡献了 2032 项 疫苗 对无症状 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染(36;45.6%)、有症状 新型冠状病毒感染(31;39.2%)、严重或危重 新型冠状病毒感染(53;67.1%)和全因 死亡率 有效性的估计(2;2.5%)。对 疫苗 跨平台、给药方案和人群的性能进行结构化比较,重点关注严重或危重的 新型冠状病毒感染 疾病。据观察,疫苗接种 对 Omicron 变体引起的严重或危重疾病有很强的保护作用,特别是对于 mRNA 疫苗。大多数结果被判断为存在中等偏倚风险(361 项,18%)或存在严重偏倚风险(1591 项,78%),主要问题是混淆。我们讨论对未来大流行防范的影响,包括标准化研究方案的重要性;疫苗接种、测试和结果之间强大的数据链接;并为不同的观察设计验证了偏倚风险框架。
  • 在 4490 名 60 岁以下符合 JN.1-疫苗 资格的参与者中,1283 名 (29%) 接种了疫苗。在随访期间,发生了 2142 例感染,其中大多数是自我报告的。疫苗 在 60 岁以下的参与者中的有效性为 16% (95%CI: -11-36),在 60 岁以上的参与者中的有效性为 13% (95%CI: 2-23)。VE 针对 KP.3.1.1 (n = 251;27%) 与 VE 针对 XEC (n = 195;5%) 没有显着差异 (OR:1.3;0.8-2.1)。
  • 感染严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的妇女的并发症发生率显着高于未感染对照,其足月和早产儿的心肌酶模式异常、新生儿肺炎、中心房间隔缺损、新生儿高胆红素血症、新生儿凝血疾病和新生儿呼吸衰竭的发病率升高。
  • This reporting window highlights the following research developments.
  • A total of 135 participants were enrolled, with over 90% infected with immune-evasive SARS-CoV-2 variants. No treatment-related adverse events or serious adverse events were reported. [10.1186/s12929-026-01284-5].
  • The review found that vaccination was strongly protective against severe or critical disease caused by the Omicron variant, with mRNA vaccines showing the highest effectiveness. [10.1016/j.vaccine.2026.128993].
  • Of 4490 JN.1-vaccine eligible participants <60 years, 1283 (29%) were vaccinated. During follow-up, 2142 infections occurred, with the majority self-reported.
  • SARS-CoV-2-infected women had significantly higher complication rates than uninfected controls, and their term and preterm infants showed elevated incidences of myocardial enzyme pattern abnormalities, neonatal pneumonia, central atrial septal defect, neonatal hyperbilirubinemia, neonatal clotting diseases, and neonatal respiratory failure.

📘 主报告：研究论文 / Primary Research

Journal: Journal of Biomedical Science  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-14  |  DOI: 10.1186/s12929-026-01284-5  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 33, 1, 83
A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

Safety and antiviral activity of inhaled siRNA SNS812 for treatment of mild-to-moderate COVID-19: a phase 2 randomized trial.

Safety and antiviral activity of inhaled siRNA SNS812 for treatment of mild-to-moderate COVID-19: a phase 2 randomized trial.
作者： Shey‐Ying Chen, Sui‐Yuan Chang, Yi-Chung Chang, Ming-Che Liu, Kuan-Yuan Chen, Jer‐Hwa Chang, Wen-Pin Tseng, Chien‐Hao Lin, Jhong-Lin Wu, Pai-Chien Chou
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
SARS-CoV-2 continues to pose a global health threat due to immune evasion and reduced effectiveness of existing therapies, creating a need for antiviral strategies effective against emerging variants. The study aimed to evaluate the safety and antiviral activity of inhaled siRNA SNS812, targeting a conserved region of the viral RNA-dependent RNA polymerase gene, in adults with mild-to-moderate COVID-19.
设计与对象
This was a phase 2, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled trial conducted in adults with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 within 3 days of symptom onset, who were randomly assigned in a 1:1:1 ratio to receive placebo or inhaled SNS812 at 100 mg or 200 mg once daily for 7 days.
核心方法
安全性是主要终点,而次要终点包括持续缓解和解决预先指定的复合目标症状和个体症状的时间。病毒学结果是探索性的,该试验在 ClinicalTrials.gov 上注册,标识符为 NCT05941793。
主要结果
共有 135 名参与者参与,其中超过 90% 的人感染了免疫逃避的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变种。没有报告与 治疗 相关的不良事件或严重不良事件。探索性分析显示,200 mg SNS812 组达到 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 抗原阴性的中位时间较短(2.9 天 vs 3.6 天;p = 0.007),病毒载量降低率更快(-0.755 vs -0.652;p = 0.040)。
解释与创新
The authors interpreted inhaled SNS812 as safe and well tolerated, with dose-dependent antiviral activity and exploratory signals of symptomatic improvement in adults with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 infected with immune-evasive variants. They highlighted the novelty of targeting a conserved viral region to address immune-evasive variants.
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,吸入 SNS812 作为 抗病毒药物 疗法的潜力,特别是针对免疫逃避的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变异,需要在包括老年人和高风险人群的更大规模、动力充足的试验中进行进一步评估。
局限与证据强度
该研究的探索性病毒学和症状终点限制了明确的结论,而且样本量小降低了普遍性。由于缺乏预先指定的主要疗效结果并且依赖于探索性分析,证据强度中等。

Safety and antiviral activity of inhaled siRNA SNS812 for treatment of mild-to-moderate COVID-19: a phase 2 randomized trial.

Authors: Shey‐Ying Chen, Sui‐Yuan Chang, Yi-Chung Chang, Ming-Che Liu, Kuan-Yuan Chen, Jer‐Hwa Chang, Wen-Pin Tseng, Chien‐Hao Lin, Jhong-Lin Wu, Pai-Chien Chou
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: SARS-CoV-2 remains a global health threat because ongoing viral evolution and immune evasion reduce the effectiveness of existing therapies. SNS812 is an inhaled small interfering RNA targeting a highly conserved region of the viral RNA-dependent RNA polymerase gene, representing a strategy that may enable broader antiviral activity against emerging variants. METHODS: In this phase 2, double-blind, randomised, placebo-controlled trial, adults with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 within 3 days of symptom onset were randomly assigned (1:1:1) to receive placebo or inhaled SNS812 (100 mg or 200 mg) once daily for 7 days (ClinicalTrials.gov identifier: NCT05941793). Safety was the primary endpoint. Secondary endpoints included the time to sustained alleviation (TTSA) and resolution (TTSR) for prespecified composite target symptoms and individual symptoms. Virological outcomes were exploratory. RESULTS: A total of 135 participants were enrolled, with more than 90% infected with immune-evasive SARS-CoV-2 variants. No treatment-related adverse events or serious adverse events were reported. In exploratory analyses, the 200 mg SNS812 group showed a shorter median time to SARS-CoV-2 antigen negativity (2.9 vs 3.6 days; p = 0.007) and a faster viral load reduction rate (- 0.755 vs - 0.652; p = 0.040), demonstrating dose-dependent virological effects. In the modified intention-to-treat population, exploratory symptom analyses showed shorter TTSA and TTSR for prespecified target symptoms with SNS812 200 mg compared with placebo (median TTSR 6.1 vs 8.1 days; adjusted hazard ratio 2.07, 95% CI 1.30-3.29; median TTSA 3.6 vs 6.5 days; adjusted hazard ratio 1.81, 95% CI 1.14-2.88). CONCLUSION: Inhaled SNS812 was safe and well tolerated and showed dose-dependent antiviral activity, with exploratory signals of symptomatic improvement in adults with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 infected with immune-evasive variants. These findings support further evaluation in larger, adequately powered trials, including older and higher-risk populations.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
SARS-CoV-2 continues to pose a global health threat due to immune evasion and reduced effectiveness of existing therapies, creating a need for antiviral strategies effective against emerging variants. The study aimed to evaluate the safety and antiviral activity of inhaled siRNA SNS812, targeting a conserved region of the viral RNA-dependent RNA polymerase gene, in adults with mild-to-moderate COVID-19.
Design and population
This was a phase 2, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled trial conducted in adults with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 within 3 days of symptom onset, who were randomly assigned in a 1:1:1 ratio to receive placebo or inhaled SNS812 at 100 mg or 200 mg once daily for 7 days.
Core methods
Safety was the primary endpoint, while secondary endpoints included time to sustained alleviation and resolution of prespecified composite target symptoms and individual symptoms. Virological outcomes were exploratory, and the trial was registered under ClinicalTrials.gov identifier NCT05941793.
Main results
A total of 135 participants were enrolled, with over 90% infected with immune-evasive SARS-CoV-2 variants. No treatment-related adverse events or serious adverse events were reported. Exploratory analyses showed that the 200 mg SNS812 group had a shorter median time to SARS-CoV-2 antigen negativity (2.9 vs 3.6 days; p = 0.007) and a faster viral load reduction rate (-0.755 vs -0.652; p = 0.040).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted inhaled SNS812 as safe and well tolerated, with dose-dependent antiviral activity and exploratory signals of symptomatic improvement in adults with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 infected with immune-evasive variants. They highlighted the novelty of targeting a conserved viral region to address immune-evasive variants.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest potential for inhaled SNS812 as an antiviral therapy, particularly against immune-evasive SARS-CoV-2 variants, warranting further evaluation in larger, adequately powered trials including older and higher-risk populations.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study’s exploratory virological and symptom endpoints limit definitive conclusions, and the small sample size reduces generalisability. The evidence strength is moderate due to the absence of prespecified primary efficacy outcomes and reliance on exploratory analyses.

在荷兰进行的一项前瞻性 队列研究 研究中,Omicron JN.1 疫苗接种 对 JN.1 衍生的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 亚变体感染的有效性

Effectiveness of Omicron JN.1 vaccination against infection with JN.1-derived SARS-CoV-2 subvariants in a prospective cohort study in the Netherlands
作者： Anne J. Huiberts, Dirk Eggink, Brechje de Gier, Thimon Karens, Hester E. de Melker, Susan J. M. Hahné, Diederick E. Grobbee, Janneke H.H.M. van de Wijgert, Susan van den Hof, Mirjam J. Knol
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在评估 JN.1 疫苗接种 在特定时期内对抗(亚)变体 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的有效性。
设计与对象
一项前瞻性 队列研究 研究在荷兰进行,包括符合 JN.1 队列研究 资格的年龄 ≥ 60 岁的参与者以及具有医疗风险状况或医护人员身份的 <60 岁参与者。在 19,349 名 60 岁以上参与者中,有 14,400 人 (74%) 接种了疫苗。
核心方法
该研究使用来自阳性自检的病毒遗传物质的完整 基因组 测序来确定感染的变异。疫苗 的有效性是使用总体感染的 Cox 回归和子变体 KP.3.1.1 和 XEC 的多项逻辑回归进行估计的,并按日历周匹配感染和未感染的参与者。模型根据年龄组、性别、教育水平、医疗风险状况和 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染史进行调整。
主要结果
在 4490 名 60 岁以下符合 JN.1-疫苗 资格的参与者中,1283 名 (29%) 接种了疫苗。在随访期间,发生了 2142 例感染,其中大多数是自我报告的。疫苗 在 60 岁以下的参与者中的有效性为 16% (95%CI: -11-36),在 60 岁以上的参与者中的有效性为 13% (95%CI: 2-23)。VE 针对 KP.3.1.1 (n = 251;27%) 与 VE 针对 XEC (n = 195;5%) 没有显着差异 (OR:1.3;0.8-2.1)。
解释与创新
观察到的 VE 估计表明对 XEC 的保护可能低于 KP.3.1.1,尽管检测这种差异的能力较低。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现有助于理解 疫苗 针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 新兴亚变体的有效性,特别是在 发病率 周期较低的情况下,并强调需要进一步研究亚变体特异性保护。
局限与证据强度
该研究是在 发病率 较低的时期进行的,这可能会限制研究结果的普遍性。检测 VE 与亚变体差异的能力较低,应谨慎解释结果。

Effectiveness of Omicron JN.1 vaccination against infection with JN.1-derived SARS-CoV-2 subvariants in a prospective cohort study in the Netherlands

Authors: Anne J. Huiberts, Dirk Eggink, Brechje de Gier, Thimon Karens, Hester E. de Melker, Susan J. M. Hahné, Diederick E. Grobbee, Janneke H.H.M. van de Wijgert, Susan van den Hof, Mirjam J. Knol
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: We estimated vaccine effectiveness (VE) of JN.1 COVID-19 vaccination against SARS-CoV-2 infection by (sub)variant between 23 September 2024 and 23 February 2025. METHODS: JN.1 vaccine-eligible participants of an ongoing prospective cohort study (VAccine Study COvid-19; VASCO) were included: individuals aged ≥60 years, and individuals aged <60 years with a medical risk condition or who were healthcare workers. In VASCO, questionnaire and serology data are regularly collected and self-tests are provided. SARS-CoV-2 infection was based on reported positive self-tests and/or anti-nucleoprotein serology results. The variant of infection was determined by whole genome sequencing of viral genetic material in positive self-tests. VE against infection was estimated using Cox regression with JN.1-vaccination as time-varying exposure, and VE against JN.1 subvariants KP.3.1.1 and XEC using multinomial logistic regression with matching of infected and uninfected participants by calendar week. Models were adjusted for age group, sex, education level, medical risk condition and SARS-CoV-2 infection history. RESULTS: Of 4490 JN.1-vaccine eligible participants <60 years, 1283 (29%) were vaccinated. Of 19,349 participants ≥60 years, 14,400 (74%) were vaccinated. During follow-up 2142 infections occurred, of which the majority was self-reported (72%). VE was 16% (95%CI: -11-36) in participants <60 years and 13% (95%CI: 2-23) in participants ≥60 years. VE against KP.3.1.1 (n = 251;27%) did not differ significantly from the VE against XEC (n = 195;5%)(OR:1.3; 0.8-2.1). CONCLUSION: We found that, during a 5-month study period with low incidence, JN.1-vaccination provided limited added protection in preventing SARS-CoV-2 infection. The observed VE estimates indicate potentially lower protection against XEC than KP.3.1.1, but the power to detect such a difference was low.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to estimate the effectiveness of JN.1 vaccination against SARS-CoV-2 infection by (sub)variant during a specific period.
Design and population
A prospective cohort study was conducted in the Netherlands, including JN.1 vaccine-eligible participants aged ≥60 years and those <60 years with medical risk conditions or healthcare worker status. Of 19,349 participants ≥60 years, 14,400 (74%) were vaccinated.
Core methods
The study used whole genome sequencing of viral genetic material from positive self-tests to determine the variant of infection. Vaccine effectiveness was estimated using Cox regression for overall infection and multinomial logistic regression for subvariants KP.3.1.1 and XEC, with matching of infected and uninfected participants by calendar week. Models were adjusted for age group, sex, education level, medical risk condition, and SARS-CoV-2 infection history.
Main results
Of 4490 JN.1-vaccine eligible participants <60 years, 1283 (29%) were vaccinated. During follow-up, 2142 infections occurred, with the majority self-reported. Vaccine effectiveness was 16% (95%CI: -11-36) in participants <60 years and 13% (95%CI: 2-23) in participants ≥60 years. VE against KP.3.1.1 (n = 251;27%) did not differ significantly from VE against XEC (n = 195;5%) (OR:1.3; 0.8-2.1).
Interpretation and novelty
The observed VE estimates suggest potentially lower protection against XEC than KP.3.1.1, although the power to detect such a difference was low.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the understanding of vaccine effectiveness against emerging subvariants of SARS-CoV-2, particularly in the context of low incidence periods, and highlight the need for further research on subvariant-specific protection.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was conducted during a period of low incidence, which may limit the generalisability of the findings. The power to detect differences in VE against subvariants was low, and the results should be interpreted with caution.

Journal: PLoS ONE  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-12  |  DOI: 10.1371/journal.pone.0355223  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 21, 8, e0355223
A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

Association between COVID-19 by variant period and delivery-related outcomes in the United States.

Association between COVID-19 by variant period and delivery-related outcomes in the United States.
作者： Deborah Kilday, Michael Korvink, Drew Tatum, Zhun Cao, Ning Rosenthal, Raymond Perigard, Ashley Finke, Richelle Marshall, Margaret Snyder, Kelly Larson
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to address the knowledge gap regarding the variation in maternal and fetal risks associated with different SARS-CoV-2 variant periods during the pandemic. The background highlights that COVID-19 has been linked to adverse obstetric outcomes, but the extent of these risks may differ across viral variant periods.
设计与对象
A retrospective cohort study was conducted using delivery-related hospitalizations from the Premier Healthcare Database, covering discharges between April 1, 2020, and March 31, 2023. The study included 2,561,615 delivery-related discharges, of which 53,226 were diagnosed with COVID-19.
核心方法
The study employed regression modeling to evaluate variant-period-specific associations between COVID-19 exposure and key outcomes, adjusting for a broad set of maternal risk factors. The analysis was based on data from the Premier Healthcare Database.
主要结果
Of the 2,561,615 delivery-related discharges, 53,226 (2.08%) were diagnosed with COVID-19. The adjusted odds ratios (aOR) for in-hospital mortality were significantly higher during the Pre-Alpha, Alpha, Delta, and first Omicron phases, with the most pronounced effect observed during the Delta period.
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 相关的变异依赖性产科风险的证据,强调了 Delta 期间严重程度的增加。这项研究提供了关于不同病毒变体如何影响母婴结局的新见解。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现有助于了解 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的变异特异性产科负担,支持有针对性的孕产妇健康 监测 和未来传染病 暴发 的临床准备。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其对行政数据的依赖而受到限制,这可能会引入偏差和信息缺失。尽管存在这些限制,但证据很充分,在多个不同时期观察到统计上显着的关联。

Association between COVID-19 by variant period and delivery-related outcomes in the United States.

Authors: Deborah Kilday, Michael Korvink, Drew Tatum, Zhun Cao, Ning Rosenthal, Raymond Perigard, Ashley Finke, Richelle Marshall, Margaret Snyder, Kelly Larson
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: COVID-19 has been associated with adverse obstetric outcomes, but the magnitude of maternal and fetal risk may have varied across viral variant periods. OBJECTIVE: To assess the association between COVID-19 infection and delivery-related in-hospital and 90-day mortality, severe maternal morbidity (SMM), fetal outcomes (fetal death and preterm birth), and a set of utilization measures (including cesarean delivery, ICU admission, costs, and hospital length of stay [LOS]) in the United States. METHODS: A retrospective cohort study was conducted using delivery-related hospitalizations discharged between April 1, 2020, and March 31, 2023, from the Premier Healthcare Database. Regression modeling was conducted to evaluate variant-period-specific associations between COVID-19 exposure and key outcomes while controlling for a broad set of maternal risk factors. RESULTS: Of the 2,561,615 evaluated delivery-related discharges, 53,226 (2.08%) were diagnosed with COVID-19. COVID-19 was associated with higher adjusted odds of in-hospital mortality across the Pre-Alpha, Alpha, Delta, and first Omicron phases (aOR [p-adj]: 13.11 [<.001], 13.03 [<.001], 29.47 [<.001], and 3.55 [.04]), respectively. Maternal COVID-19 infection was further associated with increased odds of fetal death at 20 or more weeks of gestation across the Pre-Alpha (aOR: 1.53 [1.28-1.83, < .001]) and Delta (3.37 [2.95-3.84, < .001]) phases. The odds of experiencing any SMM, ICU admission, or preterm birth were significantly higher across all phases of the pandemic, whereas the odds of cesarean delivery were significantly higher during all but the Alpha and first Omicron phases. COVID-19 infection was associated with increased LOS and hospital costs throughout all variant phases. CONCLUSIONS: COVID-19 infection during delivery was associated with higher odds of maternal, fetal, and utilization outcomes at varying stages of the pandemic, with the most notable effect during the Delta period. These findings provide insight into the variant-dependent obstetric burden of COVID-19 and for maternal health surveillance and clinical preparedness during future infectious disease outbreaks.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the knowledge gap regarding the variation in maternal and fetal risks associated with different SARS-CoV-2 variant periods during the pandemic. The background highlights that COVID-19 has been linked to adverse obstetric outcomes, but the extent of these risks may differ across viral variant periods.
Design and population
A retrospective cohort study was conducted using delivery-related hospitalizations from the Premier Healthcare Database, covering discharges between April 1, 2020, and March 31, 2023. The study included 2,561,615 delivery-related discharges, of which 53,226 were diagnosed with COVID-19.
Core methods
The study employed regression modeling to evaluate variant-period-specific associations between COVID-19 exposure and key outcomes, adjusting for a broad set of maternal risk factors. The analysis was based on data from the Premier Healthcare Database.
Main results
Of the 2,561,615 delivery-related discharges, 53,226 (2.08%) were diagnosed with COVID-19. The adjusted odds ratios (aOR) for in-hospital mortality were significantly higher during the Pre-Alpha, Alpha, Delta, and first Omicron phases, with the most pronounced effect observed during the Delta period.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as evidence of variant-dependent obstetric risks associated with SARS-CoV-2, emphasizing the increased severity during the Delta period. This study provides novel insights into how different viral variants may influence maternal and fetal outcomes.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the understanding of variant-specific obstetric burdens of SARS-CoV-2, supporting targeted maternal health surveillance and clinical preparedness during future infectious disease outbreaks.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on administrative data, which may introduce biases and missing information. Despite these limitations, the evidence is strong, with statistically significant associations observed across multiple variant periods.

Comparison of Epidemiology, Risk Factors, Clinical Presentation and Outcomes of COVID-19 and Seasonal Influenza Among Hospitalized Swiss Children: A Nationwide Prospective Surveillance Study.

Comparison of Epidemiology, Risk Factors, Clinical Presentation and Outcomes of COVID-19 and Seasonal Influenza Among Hospitalized Swiss Children: A Nationwide Prospective Surveillance Study.
作者： Petra Zimmermann, Carlos Sanchez, Michael Buettcher, Ulrich Heininger, Julia A Bielicki, for the CH-SUR Study Group, CH-SUR Study Group
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to compare the epidemiology, risk factors, clinical presentation and outcomes of children hospitalized with COVID-19 or seasonal influenza, and to identify factors associated with severe disease.
设计与对象
2018 年 12 月至 2023 年 11 月期间,在瑞士各医院进行了一项全国性前瞻性 监测 研究,比较了因实验室确诊的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 或季节性流感感染而住院的儿童和青少年(<18 岁)。
核心方法
在全国范围内的 监测 系统中前瞻性收集数据,并根据主要传播的病毒变种定义的四个时间段对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 数据进行分析:野生型 (T1)、Alpha (T2)、Delta (T3) 和 Omicron (T4)。流感数据分两个时期进行分析:“新冠疫情前”(2018年12月至2020年4月30日)和“新冠疫情期间”(2020年5月1日至2023年11月30日)。
主要结果
在 4662 名儿童中,3132 名(67.2%)因 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染住院,1530 名(32.8%)因流感住院。年龄分布因病毒和时期而异(P < 0.001):婴儿占 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 入院人数的三分之一到近一半(32.2%-46.7%),青少年在 T1 期间的贡献也显着(22.7%),而幼儿和学龄儿童因流感感染住院的比例更高。仅在感染 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的儿童中记录到嗅觉丧失(最常见于 T1:1.2%;P < 0.001)。严重症状的比例也相似,但呼​​吸窘迫在 T4 期间(12.6%)和流感期间(18.1%;P = 0.02)更常见。
解释与创新
作者解释说,与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 相比,流感与更频繁的 ICU 入院、并发症、抗生素使用和呼吸支持相关,强调需要持续的 监测 来监测不断变化的疾病模式。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现支持需要在传统高危人群之外制定更广泛的儿科流感 疫苗接种 策略,并为有关疾病管理和预防的临床和公共卫生决策提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其对 监测 系统的依赖以及可能存在未测量的混杂因素而受到限制,但由于其全国范围和前瞻性设计,证据是强有力的。

Comparison of Epidemiology, Risk Factors, Clinical Presentation and Outcomes of COVID-19 and Seasonal Influenza Among Hospitalized Swiss Children: A Nationwide Prospective Surveillance Study.

Authors: Petra Zimmermann, Carlos Sanchez, Michael Buettcher, Ulrich Heininger, Julia A Bielicki, for the CH-SUR Study Group, CH-SUR Study Group
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Although many children infected with severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus type 2 (SARS-CoV-2) or seasonal influenza virus experience a mild course of disease, both infections can lead to hospitalization and, in a subset of children, severe disease requiring intensive care unit (ICU) admission for respiratory or cardiac support. To compare the epidemiology, risk factors, clinical presentation and outcomes of children hospitalized with COVID-19 or seasonal influenza, and to identify factors associated with severe disease. METHODS: Data were collected prospectively within a nationwide surveillance system across Swiss hospitals between December 2018 and November 2023. A comparative analysis was performed among children and adolescents (<18 years) hospitalized with laboratory-confirmed SARS-CoV-2 or seasonal influenza infection. SARS-CoV-2 data were analyzed according to four time periods defined by the predominantly circulating virus variant: wild type (T1), Alpha (T2), Delta (T3) and Omicron (T4). Influenza data were analyzed across two periods: "pre-COVID" (December 2018 to April 30, 2020) and "during COVID" (May 1, 2020, to November 30, 2023). RESULTS: Of 4662 included children, 3132 (67.2%) were hospitalized with SARS-CoV-2 infection and 1530 (32.8%) with influenza. Age distribution differed by virus and period (P < 0.001): infants accounted for one-third to almost half of SARS-CoV-2 admissions (32.2%-46.7%), with adolescents also contributing notably during T1 (22.7%), whereas toddlers and school-aged children were more frequently hospitalized with influenza infection. Fever, cough and rhinitis were the most common symptoms for both viruses. Among periods with symptom data available, fever was less frequent during SARS-CoV-2 T4 (47.3%) than during earlier SARS-CoV-2 waves (62.8% in T1, 55.7% in T2 and 64.9% in T3) and was also less frequent among children with influenza during the COVID period (26.0%). Anosmia was recorded only among children with SARS-CoV-2 infection (most often during T1: 1.2%; P < 0.001). Proportions of severe symptoms were also similar, except respiratory distress being more common during T4 (12.6%) and with influenza (18.1%; P = 0.02). Acute respiratory distress syndrome was recorded only among children hospitalized with influenza and not among those hospitalized with SARS-CoV-2. ICU admissions were highest during influenza seasons (pre-COVID 10.5% and during COVID 11.8%) compared with 5.0% to 10.3% with SARS-CoV-2 (P = 0.001). Complications were more frequent in children with seasonal influenza (P < 0.001), most commonly lobar pneumonia, which occurred in up to 20% of children with influenza. During T3, deconditioning syndrome was more frequently reported (5.6%) than in other periods. Antibiotic use was higher in children with influenza (40.7% vs. Factors independently associated with severe disease were neurological, cardiovascular and other underlying conditions in children with COVID-19, and neurological and respiratory conditions in children with influenza. CONCLUSIONS: Among hospitalized children with laboratory-confirmed infection, influenza was associated with more frequent ICU admission, complications, antibiotic use and respiratory support than SARS-CoV-2. Although underlying conditions were associated with an increased risk for severe disease, most children had no preexisting underlying conditions. These findings support consideration of broader pediatric influenza vaccination strategies beyond traditional high-risk groups and the need for ongoing surveillance to monitor evolving disease patterns and inform clinical and public health decisions.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to compare the epidemiology, risk factors, clinical presentation and outcomes of children hospitalized with COVID-19 or seasonal influenza, and to identify factors associated with severe disease.
Design and population
A nationwide prospective surveillance study was conducted across Swiss hospitals between December 2018 and November 2023, comparing children and adolescents (<18 years) hospitalized with laboratory-confirmed SARS-CoV-2 or seasonal influenza infection.
Core methods
Data were collected prospectively within a nationwide surveillance system, and SARS-CoV-2 data were analyzed according to four time periods defined by the predominantly circulating virus variant: wild type (T1), Alpha (T2), Delta (T3) and Omicron (T4). Influenza data were analyzed across two periods: "pre-COVID" (December 2018 to April 30, 2020) and "during COVID" (May 1, 2020, to November 30, 2023).
Main results
Of 4662 included children, 3132 (67.2%) were hospitalized with SARS-CoV-2 infection and 1530 (32.8%) with influenza. Age distribution differed by virus and period (P < 0.001): infants accounted for one-third to almost half of SARS-CoV-2 admissions (32.2%-46.7%), with adolescents also contributing notably during T1 (22.7%), whereas toddlers and school-aged children were more frequently hospitalized with influenza infection. Anosmia was recorded only among children with SARS-CoV-2 infection (most often during T1: 1.2%; P < 0.001). Proportions of severe symptoms were also similar, except respiratory distress being more common during T4 (12.6%) and with influenza (18.1%; P = 0.02).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that influenza was associated with more frequent ICU admission, complications, antibiotic use and respiratory support than SARS-CoV-2, highlighting the need for ongoing surveillance to monitor evolving disease patterns.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings support the need for broader pediatric influenza vaccination strategies beyond traditional high-risk groups and inform clinical and public health decisions regarding disease management and prevention.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on a surveillance system and the potential for unmeasured confounding factors, but the evidence is strong due to its nationwide scope and prospective design.

Epidemiology and mortality among hospital-acquired cases of COVID-19 in the pre-Omicron and Omicron periods: A data-linkage study in Victoria, Australia.

Epidemiology and mortality among hospital-acquired cases of COVID-19 in the pre-Omicron and Omicron periods: A data-linkage study in Victoria, Australia.
作者： Lyn-Li Lim, Stephanie K Tanamas, Suresh Karunappan, Leon J Worth, Ann Bull, Elizabeth J Robinson, N Deborah Friedman
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to compare the epidemiology and mortality of hospital-acquired versus non-hospital-acquired COVID-19 infections among hospitalised patients.
设计与对象
A retrospective cohort study of COVID-19 hospitalisations in Victoria, Australia (January 2021-June 2024), using linked state surveillance systems.
核心方法
如果首次阳性检测发生在入院后 ≥8 天,则将发作归类为医院获得性。对 Omicron 之前和 Omicron 时期医院获得性和非医院获得性病例的年龄、性别、重症监护病房入住情况和 30 天 死亡率 进行了比较。
主要结果
Of 6,485 hospital-acquired and 62,670 non-hospital-acquired episodes, median age for hospital-acquired versus non-hospital-acquired cases was 77.8 versus 77.6 years before Omicron and 52.6 versus 73.1 years during Omicron. Monthly proportion of hospital-acquired cases increased during Omicron (range: 1% to 21%). Case fatality rates were higher in hospital-acquired than in non-hospital-acquired cases, with a larger difference before the Omicron (incidence rate ratio: 4.49, 95% confidence interval: 2.02, 9.99) than during the Omicron period (incidence rate ratio: 1.62, 95% confidence interval: 1.44, 1.82).
解释与创新
Hospital-acquired COVID-19 infections remain significant, associated with higher mortality rates than non-hospital-acquired infection and disproportionately affecting older patients.
科研与公卫意义
Our findings underscore the need for robust hospital COVID-19 infection prevention strategies.
局限与证据强度
该研究因其回顾性设计和对国家 监测 系统的依赖而受到限制,但证据充足,样本量大,统计分析清晰。

Epidemiology and mortality among hospital-acquired cases of COVID-19 in the pre-Omicron and Omicron periods: A data-linkage study in Victoria, Australia.

Authors: Lyn-Li Lim, Stephanie K Tanamas, Suresh Karunappan, Leon J Worth, Ann Bull, Elizabeth J Robinson, N Deborah Friedman
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVE: The aim of this study was to compare the epidemiology and mortality of hospital-acquired versus non-hospital-acquired COVID-19 infections among hospitalised patients. METHODS: We conducted a retrospective cohort study of COVID‐19 hospitalisations in Victoria, Australia (January 2021-June 2024), using linked state surveillance systems. Episodes were classified as hospital acquired if the first positive test occurred ≥8 days post admission. Age, sex, intensive care unit admission and 30‐day mortality were compared between hospital-acquired and non‐hospital-acquired cases across pre‐Omicron and Omicron periods. RESULTS: Of 6,485 hospital-acquired and 62,670 non-hospital-acquired episodes, median age for hospital-acquired versus non-hospital-acquired cases was 77.8 versus 77.6 years before Omicron and 52.6 versus 73.1 years during Omicron. Monthly proportion of hospital-acquired cases increased during Omicron (range: 1% to 21%). Case fatality rates were higher in hospital-acquired than in non-hospital-acquired cases, with a larger difference before the Omicron (incidence rate ratio: 4.49, 95% confidence interval: 2.02, 9.99) than during the Omicron period (incidence rate ratio: 1.62, 95% confidence interval: 1.44, 1.82). Intensive care unit admission was associated with 30-day mortality for hospital-acquired (adjusted odds ratio: 4.4, 95% confidence interval: 2.3-8.4) and non‐hospital-acquired cases (adjusted odds ratio: 4.0, 95% confidence interval: 3.5-4.7). CONCLUSIONS: Hospital-acquired COVID-19 infections remain significant, associated with higher mortality rates than non-hospital-acquired infection and disproportionately affecting older patients. IMPLICATIONS FOR PUBLIC HEALTH: Our findings underscore the need for robust hospital COVID-19 infection prevention strategies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to compare the epidemiology and mortality of hospital-acquired versus non-hospital-acquired COVID-19 infections among hospitalised patients.
Design and population

Core methods
Episodes were classified as hospital acquired if the first positive test occurred ≥8 days post admission. Age, sex, intensive care unit admission and 30-day mortality were compared between hospital-acquired and non-hospital-acquired cases across pre-Omicron and Omicron periods.
Main results
Of 6,485 hospital-acquired and 62,670 non-hospital-acquired episodes, median age for hospital-acquired versus non-hospital-acquired cases was 77.8 versus 77.6 years before Omicron and 52.6 versus 73.1 years during Omicron. Monthly proportion of hospital-acquired cases increased during Omicron (range: 1% to 21%). Case fatality rates were higher in hospital-acquired than in non-hospital-acquired cases, with a larger difference before the Omicron (incidence rate ratio: 4.49, 95% confidence interval: 2.02, 9.99) than during the Omicron period (incidence rate ratio: 1.62, 95% confidence interval: 1.44, 1.82).
Interpretation and novelty
Hospital-acquired COVID-19 infections remain significant, associated with higher mortality rates than non-hospital-acquired infection and disproportionately affecting older patients.
Scientific and public-health significance
Our findings underscore the need for robust hospital COVID-19 infection prevention strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its retrospective design and reliance on state surveillance systems, but the evidence is strong with a large sample size and clear statistical analyses.

Differences in health status among workers who acquired COVID-19 at work compared to workers who sustained a non-COVID-19 work-related injury or illness, 18-months post-injury/infection.

Differences in health status among workers who acquired COVID-19 at work compared to workers who sustained a non-COVID-19 work-related injury or illness, 18-months post-injury/infection.
作者： Peter M. Smith, Victoria Nadalin, Colette Severin, Nancy Carnide, Arif Jetha, Mieke Koehoorn, Cheryl Peters, Cameron Mustard
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to address concerns about the long-term health impacts of COVID-19 infections by comparing the health status of workers who acquired the virus at work with those who sustained non-COVID-19 work-related injuries, 18 months post-event.
设计与对象
A cross-sectional survey was conducted from July to October 2022, involving 1,000 workers with workers’ compensation claims, comparing seven self-reported physical and mental health outcomes between those with COVID-19 and non-COVID-19 work-related injuries.
核心方法
收集调查数据并使用潜在结果中介模型来检查与各组之间健康结果差异相关的因素。
主要结果
Workers with COVID-19 infections reported better self-rated health, physical role function, and less psychological distress compared to those with non-COVID-19 work-related injuries.
解释与创新
The findings suggest that despite initial concerns, workers with work-related COVID-19 infections may have similar or better health outcomes than those with non-COVID-19 injuries, 18 months post-event.
科研与公卫意义
这些结果可能会为公共卫生策略提供参考,以监测和支持因工作相关感染而受到长期健康影响的工人,特别是那些患有新冠肺炎后病情的工人。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于自我报告的健康结果,这可能会引入偏差,并且由于设计的观察性质,证据是温和的。

Differences in health status among workers who acquired COVID-19 at work compared to workers who sustained a non-COVID-19 work-related injury or illness, 18-months post-injury/infection.

Authors: Peter M. Smith, Victoria Nadalin, Colette Severin, Nancy Carnide, Arif Jetha, Mieke Koehoorn, Cheryl Peters, Cameron Mustard
Original abstract (English)
Objective: To compare the physical and mental health status of workers who experienced work-related COVID-19 infections versus workers with other types of work-related injuries, 18 months after the initial work absence. Methods: We collected survey data from 1,000 workers with workers’ compensation claims between July and October 2022, and examined differences in seven self-reported physical and mental health outcomes. Potential outcome mediation models examined factors associated with observed differences in health outcomes. Results: We observed better self-rated health, physical role function, and less psychological distress among respondents with COVID-19 compared to non-COVID-19 claims. Reporting a disagreement with the workers’ compensation agency partially explained these differences. Conclusions: Despite concern over the long-term health impacts of COVID-19 infections, workers with COVID-19 infections reported similar or better health than those with non-COVID-19 work-related injuries, 18 months later.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address concerns about the long-term health impacts of COVID-19 infections by comparing the health status of workers who acquired the virus at work with those who sustained non-COVID-19 work-related injuries, 18 months post-event.
Design and population
A cross-sectional survey was conducted from July to October 2022, involving 1,000 workers with workers’ compensation claims, comparing seven self-reported physical and mental health outcomes between those with COVID-19 and non-COVID-19 work-related injuries.
Core methods
Survey data were collected and potential outcome mediation models were used to examine factors associated with differences in health outcomes between the groups.
Main results
Workers with COVID-19 infections reported better self-rated health, physical role function, and less psychological distress compared to those with non-COVID-19 work-related injuries.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that despite initial concerns, workers with work-related COVID-19 infections may have similar or better health outcomes than those with non-COVID-19 injuries, 18 months post-event.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results may inform public health strategies for monitoring and supporting workers with long-term health effects following work-related infections, particularly for those with post-COVID conditions.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relies on self-reported health outcomes, which may introduce bias, and the evidence is moderate due to the observational nature of the design.

疫苗 印记导致儿童 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变异感染增加

Vaccine imprinting drives increased SARS-CoV-2 variant infection in children
作者： Timothy S. Johnston, Rahul Subramanian, Wakinyan Benhamou, Emily Howerton, Robin Schlesinger, Stephen D. Schmidt, Rachel Kazmierski, Mike Castro, Bob C. Lin, Amy Henry
摘要中文翻译病毒暴露史,尤其是首次暴露,被认为影响 疫苗 的疗效和感染易感性;然而,个人 免疫应答 与人群流行病学结果之间机制联系的证据很少。最近 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体 XFG 和 BA.3.2 的共同传播显示儿童中 BA.3.2 病例的惊人丰富。通过结合儿童和成人的流行病学模型、血清学 和单克隆抗体分析,我们证明了有效的变异特异性抗体对 疫苗接种 历史的依赖性,这可以解释出生年份对这些共循环变异的不同易感性的影响。祖先交叉反应位点 I 抗体经常中和 BA.3.2,但不能中和 XFG。相比之下,Omicron 类型特异性位点 I/III 和 III 抗体经常中和 XFG,但不能中和 BA.3.2,这揭示了中和这两种共循环菌株的能力之间的权衡。这些发现在机制上将免疫史、变异中和、抗体库和变异感染风险联系起来,并表明儿童的 疫苗接种 方案应优先考虑中和广度。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
病毒暴露史,尤其是首次暴露,被认为影响 疫苗 的疗效和感染易感性,但 疫苗 与流行病学结果之间的机制联系很少。最近 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变种 XFG 和 BA.3.2 的共同传播揭示了儿童中 BA.3.2 病例的惊人丰富。
设计与对象
该研究对儿童和成人进行了观察分析,将流行病学模型与血清学测试相结合,以评估变异特异性抗体反应。重点是这些参与者的 疫苗接种 病史和抗体滴度。
核心方法
该研究将流行病学模型与 血清学 以及儿童和成人的单克隆抗体分析相结合,以评估变异特异性抗体水平对 疫苗接种 历史的依赖性。抗体中和测定定量了血清阻断 XFG 和 BA.3.2 感染的能力。
主要结果
与其他变体相比,儿童表现出 BA.3.2 感染的显着富集,表明易感性增加。祖先交叉反应位点 I 抗体中和 BA.3.2 但不中和 XFG,而 Omicron 型特异性位点 I/III 和 III 抗体中和 XFG 但不中和 BA.3.2,这揭示了抗体特异性的权衡。
解释与创新
作者解释说,先前的 疫苗接种 历史塑造了抗体库,解释了儿童对 BA.3.2 的出生年份相关的差异易感性。他们建议儿科 疫苗接种 策略应优先考虑广泛的中和,在免疫史和变异风险之间提供机制联系。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现表明,儿童 疫苗接种 方案应旨在引发广泛中和抗体,指导 疫苗 设计和公共卫生措施,以减轻 BA.3.2 风险。了解免疫史和变异易感性之间的关系也可为 监测 和治疗监测提供信息。
局限与证据强度
证据仅限于摘要,没有详细的样本量、对照组或混杂因素评估,因此研究结果是初步的且可信度中等。需要对更大规模、控制良好的 队列研究 进行进一步研究,以确认免疫史和变异易感性之间的机制联系。

Vaccine imprinting drives increased SARS-CoV-2 variant infection in children

Authors: Timothy S. Johnston, Rahul Subramanian, Wakinyan Benhamou, Emily Howerton, Robin Schlesinger, Stephen D. Schmidt, Rachel Kazmierski, Mike Castro, Bob C. Lin, Amy Henry
Original abstract (English)
Virus exposure history, particularly first exposure, is believed to shape vaccine efficacy and infection susceptibility; however, evidence for mechanistic links between immune responses in individuals and epidemiological outcome in populations is scarce. Recent co-circulation of SARS-CoV-2 variants XFG and BA.3.2 has revealed a striking enrichment in BA.3.2 cases among children. By combining epidemiological modeling, serology and monoclonal antibody analysis in children and adults, we show the dependence of effective variant-specific antibodies on vaccination history which may explain birth-year influence on differential susceptibility to these co-circulating variants. Ancestral cross-reactive site I antibodies frequently neutralize BA.3.2, but not XFG. By contrast, Omicron type-specific site I/III and III antibodies frequently neutralize XFG but not BA.3.2, revealing a tradeoff in the ability to neutralize these two co-circulating strains. These findings mechanistically link immune history, variant neutralization, antibody repertoire and variant infection risk, and suggest that vaccination regimens in children should prioritize neutralization breadth.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Virus exposure history, especially first exposure, is believed to shape vaccine efficacy and infection susceptibility, yet mechanistic links between immune responses and epidemiological outcomes are scarce. Recent co‐circulation of SARS‐CoV‐2 variants XFG and BA.3.2 has revealed a striking enrichment of BA.3.2 cases among children.
Design and population
The study performed an observational analysis of children and adults, integrating epidemiological modeling with serological testing to assess variant‐specific antibody responses. The focus was on vaccination history and antibody titers in these participants.
Core methods
The study combined epidemiological modeling with serology and monoclonal antibody analysis in children and adults to evaluate the dependence of variant‐specific antibody levels on vaccination history. Antibody neutralization assays quantified the ability of sera to block XFG and BA.3.2 infection.
Main results
Children exhibited a marked enrichment of BA.3.2 infections compared with other variants, indicating increased susceptibility. Ancestral cross‐reactive site I antibodies neutralized BA.3.2 but not XFG, whereas Omicron‐type‐specific site I/III and III antibodies neutralized XFG but not BA.3.2, revealing a tradeoff in antibody specificity.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors explain that prior vaccination history shapes the antibody repertoire, accounting for the birth‐year–related differential susceptibility of children to BA.3.2. They propose that pediatric vaccination strategies should prioritize broad neutralization, providing a mechanistic link between immune history and variant risk.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings indicate that children’s vaccination regimens should aim to elicit broadly neutralizing antibodies, guiding vaccine design and public‐health measures to mitigate BA.3.2 risk. Understanding the relationship between immune history and variant susceptibility also informs surveillance and therapeutic monitoring.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited to an abstract with no detailed sample size, control groups, or confounder assessment, making the findings preliminary and of moderate confidence. Further studies with larger, well‐controlled cohorts are needed to confirm the mechanistic links between immune history and variant susceptibility.

英格兰在和后续疫苗接种推广期间,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染后患有严重精神疾病人群的死亡率不平等:一项回顾性全国范围关联队列研究研究

Inequalities in mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection for people with severe mental illness during and following vaccination roll-out in England: a retrospective, whole-country linked cohort study.
作者： Natasha Chilman, Laia Bécares, Alexandru Dregan, Amy Ronaldson, Jadene Lewis, Mehrdad A. Mizani, Jayati Das-Munshi, Jayati Das‐Munshi, CVD-COVID-UK Consortium
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
在大流行高峰期间感染 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 后,患有严重精神疾病 (SMI) 的人经历了更高的 死亡率,但尚不清楚在 疫苗接种 推出后这种风险是否持续存在。该研究通过检查 SMI 患者中过多的 死亡率 是否在 疫苗接种 推出定义的大流行阶段发生变化,解决了知识差距。
设计与对象
This retrospective, whole-country cohort study used linked electronic health records from England covering over 99% of the population registered to primary care. The study population included 13,463,945 people with COVID-19 between January 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with data on deaths and vaccination status.
核心方法
Cox regression models estimated differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality between people with and without SMI, stratified by pandemic stages defined by WHO policy and UK vaccination roll-out. SMI was defined as schizophrenia, schizoaffective disorder, bipolar disorder, or other affective and non-affective disorders with psychosis.
主要结果
People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio 1.56, 95% CI 1.53–1.59). The elevated risk was present in stage 1 (aHR 1.27, 1.22–1.32) and increased in stages 2 (aHR 1.56, 1.51–1.61) and 3 (aHR 1.55, 1.50–1.59). By study end, fewer people with SMI were fully vaccinated (79.4%) compared with those without SMI (87.2%).
解释与创新
The authors interpreted the findings as showing that excess mortality in people with SMI persisted throughout the pandemic despite vaccination, though vaccination partially attenuated COVID-19 mortality risk (aHR 1.25, 1.17–1.34). The novelty lies in quantifying persistent disparities across pandemic stages using whole-country linked data.
科研与公卫意义
研究结果强调,需要采取有针对性的公共卫生和临床策略,以减少 SMI 患者过多的 死亡率 并提高 疫苗接种 的吸收率,特别是考虑到他们在所有大流行阶段的风险都较高。
局限与证据强度
该研究对常规健康记录的依赖可能会导致错误分类或残留混杂,并且在没有全民医疗保健的情况下的普遍性尚不确定。由于观察设计和潜在的未测量混杂因素,证据强度中等。

Inequalities in mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection for people with severe mental illness during and following vaccination roll-out in England: a retrospective, whole-country linked cohort study.

Authors: Natasha Chilman, Laia Bécares, Alexandru Dregan, Amy Ronaldson, Jadene Lewis, Mehrdad A. Mizani, Jayati Das-Munshi, Jayati Das‐Munshi, CVD-COVID-UK Consortium
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: People with severe mental illness (SMI) had a higher risk of death following SARS-CoV-2 infection compared with people without SMI during the COVID-19 pandemic peak. It is unclear whether this risk reduced in later stages after vaccination roll-out. We aimed to assess all-cause and cause-specific mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection in people with or without SMI across pandemic stages, using whole-country data, while accounting for sociodemographic and clinical characteristics. METHODS: In this retrospective, whole-country cohort study, we analysed linked electronic health records for all people in England registered to primary care who were alive in November, 2019 (>99% of the population). The study population included people who had COVID-19 between Jan 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with information on deaths and vaccination. Data extracted from the NHS England Secure Data Environment provided through the British Heart Foundation Data Science Centre CVD-COVID-UK-COVID-IMPACT Consortium linked datasets were curated to form a dataset in July, 2024. Cox regression estimated differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality. Analyses were stratified by pandemic stages, defined by WHO policy and UK Government vaccination roll-out: Jan 31-Sept 30, 2020 (stage 1); Oct 1, 2020-July 18, 2021 (stage 2, vaccination roll-out); and July 19, 2021-June 30, 2023 (stage 3, post-vaccination roll-out). SMI comprised schizophrenia, schizoaffective disorder, bipolar disorder, or other affective and non-affective disorder with psychosis. A patient and public involvement panel was consulted during design phases of the study. FINDINGS: 13 463 945 people with COVID-19 were identified, of whom 160 190 (1·19%) had SMI. People with SMI were older [mean age [SD], SMI 49·7 years [17·9], non-SMI 45·0 years [17·6]), had a similar sex split (females SMI vs non-SMI: 56·3% vs 56·6%); and were mostly White British (77·3% or 76·3%). People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio [aHR] 1·56, 95% CI 1·53-1·59). All-cause mortality risk in people with SMI was elevated in stage 1 (aHR 1·27, 95% CI 1·22-1·32), but larger in the stage 2 (aHR 1·56, 1·51-1·61) and stage 3 (aHR 1·55, 1·50-1·59). Vaccination prevalence was initially higher for people with SMI (in stage 2: 112 795 [73·2%, 95% CI 72·9-73·4], compared with people without SMI, 8 852 970 [67·4%, 67·3-67·4]). By study end, fewer people with SMI were fully vaccinated (127 145 [79·4%, 79·2-79·6]), compared with people without SMI (11 605 915 [87·2%, 87·2-87·3]. Higher COVID-19 mortality risk in people with SMI persisted but was partially attenuated by vaccination (aHR 1·25, 1·17-1·34). INTERPRETATION: Excess mortality in people with SMI persisted throughout the COVID-19 pandemic. Preventive public health and clinical strategies are needed to address excess mortality and lower vaccination. FUNDING: Health Foundation-Academy of Medical Sciences.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
People with severe mental illness (SMI) experienced higher mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection during pandemic peaks, but it was unclear whether this risk persisted after vaccination roll-out. The study addressed a knowledge gap by examining whether excess mortality in people with SMI changed across pandemic stages defined by vaccination roll-out.
Design and population
This retrospective, whole-country cohort study used linked electronic health records from England covering over 99% of the population registered to primary care. The study population included 13,463,945 people with COVID-19 between January 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with data on deaths and vaccination status.
Core methods
Cox regression models estimated differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality between people with and without SMI, stratified by pandemic stages defined by WHO policy and UK vaccination roll-out. SMI was defined as schizophrenia, schizoaffective disorder, bipolar disorder, or other affective and non-affective disorders with psychosis.
Main results
People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio 1.56, 95% CI 1.53–1.59). The elevated risk was present in stage 1 (aHR 1.27, 1.22–1.32) and increased in stages 2 (aHR 1.56, 1.51–1.61) and 3 (aHR 1.55, 1.50–1.59). By study end, fewer people with SMI were fully vaccinated (79.4%) compared with those without SMI (87.2%).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as showing that excess mortality in people with SMI persisted throughout the pandemic despite vaccination, though vaccination partially attenuated COVID-19 mortality risk (aHR 1.25, 1.17–1.34). The novelty lies in quantifying persistent disparities across pandemic stages using whole-country linked data.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings highlight the need for targeted public health and clinical strategies to reduce excess mortality and improve vaccination uptake among people with SMI, particularly given their elevated risk across all pandemic stages.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study’s reliance on routine health records may introduce misclassification or residual confounding, and generalisability to settings without universal healthcare is uncertain. The evidence strength is moderate due to the observational design and potential unmeasured confounding.

第一代单价 疫苗 在中国阻断了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的 Delta 和 Omicron 变体的 传播

First-generation monovalent vaccines blocked transmission of Delta and Omicron variants of SARS-CoV-2 in China
作者： Tim K. Tsang, Mengmeng Ma, Can Wang, Hongyan Wang, Zhicong Yang, Xiaotong Huang, Ming-Jin Liu, Yi Yin, Yushan Liu, Xiaoning Li
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在评估第一代单价灭活 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗 在降低感染者的传染性和阻止 传播 与密切接触者的接触方面的有效性,因为它们在 传播 预防中的作用仍知之甚少。
设计与对象
该研究使用了广州和扬州的达美暴发(2021年5月至6月和2021年7月至8月)和广州的Omicron 暴发(2022年4月)的接触者追踪数据,分析了不同接触环境中的323名原发病例和53,895名密切接触者。
核心方法
研究人员应用基于个体的贝叶斯 传播 模型来估计 疫苗 在降低易感性和传染性方面的有效性,并使用详细的接触者追踪数据调整三级 传播、病例输入和暴露史。
主要结果
研究发现,没有证据表明 疫苗 对降低易感性 (VES) 有效,但 疫苗 对降低 Delta 和 Omicron 变种的感染性 (VEI) 具有显着效果,≥2 剂量分别与 45% 和 54% 的感染性降低相关。
解释与创新
作者解释说,第一代单价灭活 疫苗 降低了接种疫苗的个体的传染性,从而提供了针对新变异的社区级保护,这是群体免疫的一个关键但尚未得到充分研究的方面。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,这些 疫苗 可以通过减少感染个体的传染性来抑制社区 传播,这对公共卫生策略和 疫苗 部署具有重要意义。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其对接触者追踪数据的依赖以及可能存在未测量的混杂因素而受到限制,但鉴于样本量大和严格的统计模型,证据是强有力的。

First-generation monovalent vaccines blocked transmission of Delta and Omicron variants of SARS-CoV-2 in China

Authors: Tim K. Tsang, Mengmeng Ma, Can Wang, Hongyan Wang, Zhicong Yang, Xiaotong Huang, Ming-Jin Liu, Yi Yin, Yushan Liu, Xiaoning Li
Original abstract (English)
While effectiveness of monovalent inactivated SARS-CoV-2 vaccines on preventing infection and severe disease has been well-characterized, their effectiveness on reducing infectivity of infected individuals and thereby blocking onward transmission to close contacts remains poorly understood. Leveraging contact-tracing data obtained during the Delta outbreaks in Guangzhou (May–June 2021) and Yangzhou (July–August 2021), as well as the Omicron outbreak in Guangzhou (April 2022), we used individual-based Bayesian transmission models to estimate vaccine effectiveness on reducing susceptibility (VES) and infectivity (VEI) across various contact settings, adjusting for tertiary transmission, case importation, and exposure history. Results: Based on 323 primary cases and 53,895 close contacts, we find no evidence for VES (comparing 0, 1, and ≥2 doses) but significant VEI (comparing ≤1 vs. ≥2 doses) against both Delta and Omicron variants. Compared to ≤1 dose of inactivated monovalent vaccines, ≥2 doses are associated with 45% (95% Credible Interval [CrI]: 15%, 66%) lower infectivity for the Delta variant and 54% (95% CrI: 34%–80%) for the Omicron variant respectively. Household and entertainment are associated with the highest baseline transmission risks. The most susceptible age group is older adults (60+ years) during the Delta outbreaks but children (0–19 years) during the Omicron outbreak. First-generation monovalent inactivated vaccines offer sustainable community-level protection against novel variants by reducing infectivity of infected individuals, representing a critical but understudied component of the herd immunity. Inactivated COVID-19 vaccines are known to prevent severe disease, but their ability to reduce how contagious infected people are remains poorly understood. Using detailed contact-tracing data from Delta and Omicron outbreaks in Guangzhou and Yangzhou, China (2021-2022), we used statistical models to estimate vaccine effects on both susceptibility to infection and infectivity of infected individuals. We found that while inactivated vaccines provided minimal protection against getting infected, they substantially reduced onward transmission by 45% for Delta and 54% for Omicron among individuals with two or more doses. Households and entertainment showed the highest transmission risk. These findings indicate that first-generation inactivated vaccines can suppress community transmission by reducing how infectious vaccinated cases are. use individual-based Bayesian transmission models with contact-tracing data from Delta and Omicron outbreaks in China. They find that first-generation monovalent inactivated vaccines provide minimal protection against infection but reduce onward transmission by 45-54%.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to evaluate the effectiveness of first-generation monovalent inactivated SARS-CoV-2 vaccines in reducing infectivity of infected individuals and blocking onward transmission to close contacts, as their role in transmission prevention remains poorly understood.
Design and population
The study used contact-tracing data from Delta outbreaks in Guangzhou and Yangzhou (May–June 2021 and July–August 2021) and the Omicron outbreak in Guangzhou (April 2022), analyzing 323 primary cases and 53,895 close contacts across various contact settings.
Core methods
The researchers applied individual-based Bayesian transmission models to estimate vaccine effectiveness on reducing susceptibility and infectivity, adjusting for tertiary transmission, case importation, and exposure history using detailed contact-tracing data.
Main results
The study found no evidence for vaccine effectiveness on reducing susceptibility (VES) but significant vaccine effectiveness on reducing infectivity (VEI) against both Delta and Omicron variants, with ≥2 doses associated with 45% and 54% lower infectivity respectively.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that first-generation monovalent inactivated vaccines reduce the infectivity of vaccinated individuals, thereby offering community-level protection against novel variants, which is a critical but understudied aspect of herd immunity.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that these vaccines can suppress community transmission by reducing the contagiousness of infected individuals, which has important implications for public health strategies and vaccine deployment.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on contact-tracing data and the potential for unmeasured confounding factors, but the evidence is strong given the large sample size and rigorous statistical modeling.

废水 监测 无需事先进行谱系分类,可进行可靠的实时 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体识别

Wastewater surveillance without prior lineage classification for reliable real time SARS-CoV-2 variant identification
作者： Raisa Kociurzynski, Sandra Reuter, Tjibbe Donker
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study addresses the need for rapid, real-time identification of emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants during the COVID-19 pandemic, where traditional methods relying on prior lineage classification limit the speed of variant detection.
设计与对象
该方法适用于从服务于瑞士阿尔腾莱茵、圣加仑、日内瓦和苏黎世流域的 治疗 工厂收集的废水样本。
核心方法
仅当样本的读取深度高于 40 且 基因组 覆盖率至少为 90% 时才包含在内。对两个时间段内汇集群体内的遗传多样性进行比较,以评估病毒组成的变化。
主要结果
该方法能够以合理的精度检测与已知关注变体和 Omicron 变体的出现相对应的遗传群体的变化,而无需事先进行谱系分类。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为证明,与临床样本相比,他们的方法克服了废水中固有的高基因组噪音,为可靠、实时的变异识别提供了一种新工具。
科研与公卫意义
该方法可以通过及时检测废水中新出现的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体来为公共卫生 暴发 提供信息,从而有可能改善资源分配和 暴发 响应。
局限与证据强度
证据仅限于抽象水平的数据,缺乏详细的方法学验证或更广泛的普遍性,需要谨慎解释和进一步研究。

Wastewater surveillance without prior lineage classification for reliable real time SARS-CoV-2 variant identification

Authors: Raisa Kociurzynski, Sandra Reuter, Tjibbe Donker
Original abstract (English)
Abstract The COVID-19 pandemic remains paradigmatic for the urgency of identifying emerging variants of rapidly mutating viruses in near real time. Grasping the infection dynamics enables better management of public health measures, including the timely allocation of resources. Wastewater surveillance has proven effective in estimating infection incidence and detecting variants in particular if testing rates declined due to milder disease manifestations. However, current methods typically rely on the prior classification of SARS-CoV-2 lineages or their signature mutations, which hampers the speed of variants detection. We present an alternative method that overcomes this limitation by identifying genetic changes in the viral population over time without requiring prior lineage classification. This approach was applied to wastewater samples from plants covering Swiss catchments in Altenrhein, St. Gallen, Geneva, and Zurich. To address noise, only samples with read depths above 40 and genome coverage of at least 90% were included. Genetic diversity within pooled populations over two time periods was compared to assess changes in viral composition. Application of this novel method enabled detection of shifts in genetic populations that corresponded to the emergence of known variants of concern and of the Omicron variant with reasonable precision without prior lineage classification. Notably the approach overcame the inherently high genomic noise in wastewater compared to clinical samples. In summary, we introduce a valuable tool for reliable, real time predictions for the emergence of potentially threatening virus variants from waste water samples that overcomes the need for prior lineage classification and high patient samples.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addresses the need for rapid, real-time identification of emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants during the COVID-19 pandemic, where traditional methods relying on prior lineage classification limit the speed of variant detection.
Design and population
The method was applied to wastewater samples collected from treatment plants serving Swiss catchments in Altenrhein, St. Gallen, Geneva, and Zurich.
Core methods
Samples were included only if they had read depths above 40 and genome coverage of at least 90%. Genetic diversity within pooled populations was compared over two time periods to assess changes in viral composition.
Main results
The approach enabled detection of shifts in genetic populations that corresponded to the emergence of known variants of concern and the Omicron variant with reasonable precision without prior lineage classification.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the findings as demonstrating that their method overcomes the inherently high genomic noise in wastewater compared to clinical samples, providing a novel tool for reliable, real-time variant identification.
Scientific and public-health significance
This method may inform public health surveillance by enabling timely detection of emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants from wastewater, potentially improving resource allocation and outbreak response.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited to abstract-level data and lacks detailed methodological validation or broader generalizability, warranting cautious interpretation and further study.

Is early combination therapy associated with lower risk of respiratory failure in severely immunocompromised hosts with COVID-19? a target trial emulation study from the omicron-dominant era.

Is early combination therapy associated with lower risk of respiratory failure in severely immunocompromised hosts with COVID-19? a target trial emulation study from the omicron-dominant era.
作者： Bavaro DF, L. Diella, A. Belati, C. Pellegrino, Giuseppina De Vita, Giuseppe Bruno, M. Poliseno, Bottalico IF, Ieva FRP, Federica De Gregorio
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to evaluate whether early combination therapy is associated with a lower risk of respiratory failure in severely immunocompromised hosts with COVID-19, as the optimal treatment strategy remains uncertain.
设计与对象
This pragmatic target trial emulation study used observational data collected between 01/01/2022 and 31/03/2024 in 4 tertiary-care hospitals, focusing on immunocompromised patients with mild/moderate COVID-19.
核心方法
该研究使用观察数据比较了 抗病毒药物 (DAA) 或单克隆抗体 (MoAb) 的单一疗法与 MoAb 加 DAA 的联合疗法。
主要结果
在纳入的 303 名免疫功能低下患者中,99 名 (33%) 达到了呼吸衰竭的主要结局,联合治疗组观察到 发病率 较低(17% vs. 17%)。
解释与创新
The authors interpreted the findings as suggesting that combination therapy may be associated with a reduced risk of respiratory failure in immunocompromised patients with COVID-19, offering a potential treatment strategy.
科研与公卫意义
These findings may inform treatment strategies for immunocompromised individuals with COVID-19, particularly in settings where combination therapy is feasible.
局限与证据强度
该研究基于观察数据,可能会引入偏差并限制因果推断,但由于样本量大和结果定义明确,证据有力。

Is early combination therapy associated with lower risk of respiratory failure in severely immunocompromised hosts with COVID-19? a target trial emulation study from the omicron-dominant era.

Authors: Bavaro DF, L. Diella, A. Belati, C. Pellegrino, Giuseppina De Vita, Giuseppe Bruno, M. Poliseno, Bottalico IF, Ieva FRP, Federica De Gregorio
Original abstract (English)
Introduction COVID-19 is still a potentially serious infection in immunocompromised individuals, with a significant risk of respiratory failure; nevertheless, the optimal treatment strategy is uncertain. Herein, the impact of combination therapy versus monotherapy on risk of respiratory failure was evaluated. Methods This study is a pragmatic target trial emulation performed on observational data collected between 01/01/2022 and 31/03/2024 in 4 tertiary-care hospitals. Patients immunocompromised patients with mild/moderate COVID-19. Intervention (arm A) monotherapy with antivirals (DAAs) or monoclonal antibodies (MoAbs) versus (arm B) combination therapy of MoAbs plus DAAs. Primary endpoint COVID-19-related acute respiratory failure on day 28 post-randomization. Secondary aims: adverse events to therapy, 90-day mortality, 90-day COVID-19 recurrence/complications, and duration of viral shedding. Results Overall, 1,035 subjects were screened, and 303 immunocompromised patients were included; main immunodeficiencies were hematologic cancer (71%), exposure to anti-CD20 (36%), solid organ transplantation (15%) and solid cancer (13%). After enrollment, 189 and 114 received mono- or combination therapy, respectively. Of them, 99 patients (33%) met the primary outcome, with a lower incidence in combination therapy group (17% vs. 42%, p Conclusions In immunocompromised patients with COVID-19, combination therapy appears associated with a lower risk of respiratory failure and may be preferred, when possible, over monotherapy.

Question and background
The study aimed to evaluate whether early combination therapy is associated with a lower risk of respiratory failure in severely immunocompromised hosts with COVID-19, as the optimal treatment strategy remains uncertain.
Design and population
This pragmatic target trial emulation study used observational data collected between 01/01/2022 and 31/03/2024 in 4 tertiary-care hospitals, focusing on immunocompromised patients with mild/moderate COVID-19.
Core methods
The study compared monotherapy with antivirals (DAAs) or monoclonal antibodies (MoAbs) to combination therapy of MoAbs plus DAAs using observational data.

Of the 303 immunocompromised patients included, 99 (33%) met the primary outcome of respiratory failure, with a lower incidence observed in the combination therapy group (17% vs.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as suggesting that combination therapy may be associated with a reduced risk of respiratory failure in immunocompromised patients with COVID-19, offering a potential treatment strategy.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings may inform treatment strategies for immunocompromised individuals with COVID-19, particularly in settings where combination therapy is feasible.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on observational data, which may introduce bias and limit causal inference, but the evidence is strong due to the large sample size and clear outcome definitions.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 ORF8调节上呼吸道炎症反应以促进传播

SARS-CoV-2 ORF8 modulates the upper respiratory tract inflammatory response to facilitate transmission
作者： Grace O. Ciabattoni, Stacey Bartlett, Marisa E. McGrath, Matthew B. Frieman, Meike Dittmann, Mila B. Ortigoza
摘要中文翻译摘要 呼吸道病毒利用 宿主 免疫应答 来促进 传播 的能力是流行病的一个决定性特征。由于其持久的 进化 和抵抗不断变化的免疫防御的能力,进化 仍然是全球主要的公共卫生威胁。尽管 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 刺突蛋白 的免疫逃避特性已得到很好的表征,但其他病毒蛋白对 传播 的贡献仍然知之甚少。在这里,我们使用婴儿小鼠模型来定义辅助蛋白 ORF8 在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 传播中的作用。我们证明 ORF8 支持有效的上呼吸道 (URT) 感染、传染性病毒脱落和 宿主 到 宿主 传播。与感染同基因祖先菌株rWA-1(含有完整ORF8)的小鼠相比,感染缺乏ORF8(rΔORF8)的重组严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2菌株的小鼠从URT组织和鼻分泌物中回收的传染性病毒较少,传播效率也较低。编码天然存在的 ORF8 突变 的重组病毒表现出独特的 传播 表型,ORF8 缺陷型病毒类似于 rΔORF8。与 ORF8 缺陷病毒相比,感染 ORF8 充足的病毒可诱导更多的巨噬细胞募集、炎症细胞因子产生和 I 型干扰素 (IFN-I) 信号传导程序。鼻内补充 IFNβ 部分恢复了 rΔORF8 感染小鼠的 URT 脱落,并将 传播 拯救为接触者,而阻断 rWA-1 感染的指数小鼠中的 I 型干扰素受体(IFNAR)则减少了接触感染和 传播。总之,这些发现表明 ORF8 通过参与 URT 中与 IFN-I 相关的炎症和分泌程序来促进 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 传播,该程序支持病毒从受感染的 宿主 中脱落。这些数据将 ORF8 确定为 宿主 粘膜反应的病毒决定因素,从而促进传染性。重要性 高效的 宿主 到 宿主 传播 是呼吸道病毒成功的基础。尽管 刺突蛋白 研究主要集中在 刺突蛋白 上,但辅助蛋白也可以塑造病毒适应性和传播。我们之前确定 ORF8 是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 传播 的决定因素。在这里,我们表明 ORF8 通过参与 URT 中 IFN-I 相关的炎症和分泌反应来促进 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染性病毒脱落和 传播。这些发现揭示了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 辅助蛋白如何利用粘膜 抗病毒药物 反应来增加 宿主 传染性。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究解释了为什么尽管存在免疫压力,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 仍继续对全球公共卫生构成威胁,重点关注了 传播 中除了已明确表征的 刺突蛋白 之外的辅助蛋白的作用,人们对其知之甚少。
设计与对象
作者使用婴儿小鼠模型研究了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 辅助蛋白 ORF8 对上呼吸道感染、脱落和 传播 的贡献。
核心方法
使用带有和不带有ORF8的重组严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2菌株来感染小鼠,并测量从上呼吸道组织和鼻分泌物中排出的传染性病毒以及宿主到宿主 传播的效率。
主要结果
与感染含有完整 ORF8 的同基因祖先株 rWA-1 的小鼠相比,感染 ORF8 缺陷病毒 (rΔORF8) 的小鼠从上呼吸道组织和鼻分泌物中回收的感染性病毒较少,传播效率也较低。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,ORF8 通过参与上呼吸道中支持病毒脱落的 I 型干扰素相关炎症和分泌程序来促进 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 传播。
科研与公卫意义
该研究确定 ORF8 是粘膜 免疫应答 的病毒决定因素,可增强传染性,这表明减少 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 传播的干预措施的潜在目标。
局限与证据强度
使用婴儿小鼠模型和仅摘要证据限制了对人类 传播 动力学的直接转化,需要对研究结果进行谨慎解释。

SARS-CoV-2 ORF8 modulates the upper respiratory tract inflammatory response to facilitate transmission

Authors: Grace O. Ciabattoni, Stacey Bartlett, Marisa E. McGrath, Matthew B. Frieman, Meike Dittmann, Mila B. Ortigoza
Original abstract (English)
Abstract The ability of respiratory viruses to exploit host immune responses to promote transmission is a defining feature of pandemics. SARS-CoV-2 remains a major global public health threat because of its persistent evolution and capacity to counteract evolving immune defenses. Although the immune evasion properties of the SARS-CoV-2 Spike protein are well characterized, the contributions of other viral proteins to transmission remain poorly understood. Here, we used the infant mouse model to define the role of the accessory protein ORF8 in SARS-CoV-2 spread. We demonstrate that ORF8 supports efficient upper respiratory tract (URT) infection, infectious virus shedding, and host-to-host transmission. Mice infected with a recombinant SARS-CoV-2 strain lacking ORF8 (rΔORF8) had less infectious virus recovered from URT tissues and nasal secretions and transmitted less efficiently than mice infected with the isogenic ancestral strain rWA-1, which contains an intact ORF8. Recombinant viruses encoding naturally occurring ORF8 mutations exhibited distinct transmission phenotypes, with ORF8-deficient viruses resembling rΔORF8. Infection with ORF8-sufficient viruses induced greater macrophage recruitment, inflammatory cytokine production, and type-I interferon (IFN-I) signaling programs than ORF8-deficient viruses. Intranasal IFNβ supplementation partially restored URT shedding by rΔORF8-infected mice and rescued transmission to contacts, whereas blockade of the type I interferon receptor (IFNAR) in rWA-1-infected index mice reduced contact infection and transmission. Together, these findings demonstrate that ORF8 promotes SARS-CoV-2 transmission by engaging an IFN-I-associated inflammatory and secretory program in the URT that supports virus shedding from the infected host. These data identify ORF8 as a viral determinant of host mucosal responses that promote contagiousness. Importance Efficient host-to-host transmission underlies the success of respiratory viruses. Although SARS-CoV-2 research has largely focused on the Spike protein, accessory proteins can also shape viral fitness and spread. We previously identified ORF8 as a determinant of SARS-CoV-2 transmission. Here we show that ORF8 promotes SARS-CoV-2 infectious viral shedding and transmission by engaging IFN-I-associated inflammatory and secretory responses in the URT. These findings reveal how a SARS-CoV-2 accessory protein can exploit mucosal antiviral responses to increase host contagiousness.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addresses why SARS-CoV-2 continues to pose a global public health threat despite immune pressure, focusing on the poorly understood role of accessory proteins in transmission beyond the well-characterized Spike protein.
Design and population
The authors used the infant mouse model to investigate the contribution of the SARS-CoV-2 accessory protein ORF8 to upper respiratory tract infection, shedding, and transmission.
Core methods
Recombinant SARS-CoV-2 strains with and without ORF8 were used to infect mice, and infectious virus shedding from upper respiratory tract tissues and nasal secretions was measured alongside host-to-host transmission efficiency.
Main results
Mice infected with ORF8-deficient virus (rΔORF8) had less infectious virus recovered from upper respiratory tract tissues and nasal secretions and transmitted less efficiently than mice infected with the isogenic ancestral strain rWA-1 containing intact ORF8.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings demonstrate that ORF8 promotes SARS-CoV-2 transmission by engaging type I interferon-associated inflammatory and secretory programs in the upper respiratory tract that support virus shedding.
Scientific and public-health significance
The study identifies ORF8 as a viral determinant of mucosal immune responses that enhance contagiousness, suggesting potential targets for interventions to reduce SARS-CoV-2 spread.
Limitations and evidence strength
The use of an infant mouse model and abstract-only evidence limits direct translation to human transmission dynamics, warranting cautious interpretation of the findings.
