Health outcomes and social determinants in people living with HIV in Sweden : insights from the COVID-19 era and beyond

Health outcomes and social determinants in people living with HIV in Sweden : insights from the COVID-19 era and beyond
作者： Isabela Killander Möller
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study addressed persistent disparities in health outcomes among people with HIV (PWH) despite effective antiretroviral therapy, focusing on the contribution of social determinants and the impact of COVID-19. It aimed to clarify how socioeconomic factors influence the excess risk of age-related comorbidities and COVID-19 outcomes in PWH compared with people without HIV.
设计与对象
Four nationwide register-based studies were conducted in Sweden using linked data from virtually the entire adult population or large samples. Study I included 64,815 individuals hospitalised with COVID-19 (121 PWH) from February 2020 to October 2021. Study II compared 1,348 PWH with 1,669,389 people without HIV for COVID-19 hospitalisation from January 2021 to February 2023. Study III assessed COVID-19 vaccination uptake among 7,668 PWH and 8,168,340 people without HIV. Study IV evaluated renal, cardiovascular events, and mortality after type 2 diabetes diagnosis in 350 PWH versus 311,668 people without HIV (2010–2024).
核心方法
分别使用逻辑回归和 Cox 比例风险模型估计优势比 (OR) 和风险比 (HR)。根据人口统计、社会经济因素、合并症和 疫苗接种 状态进行调整的分析。研究 II 按 疫苗接种 状态和大流行时期(Omicron 之前、公开测试期间的 Omicron、公开测试后的 Omicron)进行分层。研究 III 使用修正的泊松回归来估计 疫苗接种 摄取的风险比。
主要结果
In Study I, the unadjusted OR for severe COVID-19 was lower in PWH (OR 0.60, 95% CI 0.34–0.94), but adjustment for age and sex eliminated the difference (adjOR 0.70, 95% CI 0.43–1.26). Study II found increased unadjusted ORs for COVID-19 hospitalisation in unvaccinated or twice-vaccinated PWH, but not after adjustment; during Omicron with public testing, adjusted ORs were significantly higher in PWH. Study III showed lower unadjusted 3-dose vaccination uptake in PWH (RR 0.90, 95% CI 0.88–0.91), but higher adjusted uptake (adjRR 1.17, 95% CI 1.15–1.19), except in those aged ≥65 years (adjRR 0.91, 95% CI 0.85–0.96). Study IV reported a significantly increased risk of major adverse kidney events in PWH with T2DM (adjHR 2.04, 95% CI 1.57–2.65).
解释与创新
The findings suggest that PWH with access to ART and viral suppression do not have a higher risk of severe COVID-19 than people without HIV, though temporal variations during Omicron highlight potential testing biases. Lower vaccination uptake in PWH appears largely explained by migrant status, while frequent healthcare contact may facilitate vaccination except in older PWH. The increased risk of renal events in PWH with T2DM underscores the need for targeted monitoring.
科研与公卫意义
结果表明,解决社会经济差异,特别是移民人口中的社会经济差异,可能会降低艾滋病毒感染者过度合并症的风险。对于患有 T2DM 的 PWH,需要加强肾脏监测。疫苗接种 计划应优先考虑移民群体,以提高吸收率。
局限与证据强度
抗逆转录病毒治疗效果的详细探索受到感染者中少数事件的限制,限制了因果推断。基于寄存器的设计可能会引入残余混杂和错误分类。尽管存在这些限制,但全国范围和对多种混杂因素的调整增强了证据,值得适度的信心。

Health outcomes and social determinants in people living with HIV in Sweden : insights from the COVID-19 era and beyond

Authors: Isabela Killander Möller
Original abstract (English)
People with HIV (PWH) experience improved health and quality of life in the era of contemporary antiretroviral treatment (ART), and their life expectancy in high- income countries approaches that of people without HIV. However, disparities remain. PWH have been shown to face an increased risk and earlier onset of age- related comorbidities, even with access to effective ART and viral suppression. The comorbidity burden has been attributed in part to long-term effects of life- long ART and to residual low-grade inflammation and immune dysfunction that persist despite ART. Furthermore, PWH in high-income countries such as Sweden have lower socioeconomic status, including lower education and income, and approximately 60% are migrants. These social determinants are themselves associated with a higher risk of age-related comorbidities and may contribute to the excess risk observed among PWH. The extent to which these social factors contribute, however, has been less well studied and is important to clarify in order to target interventions effectively. The overall aim of the thesis was to investigate the risk of comorbidities, with a focus on COVID-19 and type 2 diabetes mellitus (T2DM), in PWH compared with people without HIV. Through linkage of Swedish nationwide registers across four studies, these outcomes were examined in virtually the entire adult Swedish population or a sample of it, while accounting for demographics, socioeconomic factors, and underlying comorbidities. Study I assessed the risk of severe COVID-19 (intensive care admission or mortality) among individuals hospitalised with COVID-19 by HIV status during the first phase of the pandemic (February 2020 to October 2021). In total, 64,815 individuals were hospitalised with COVID-19, of whom 121 were PWH. Odds ratios (OR) were estimated using logistic regression. The unadjusted OR of severe COVID-19 was lower among PWH compared with people without HIV (OR 0.60, 95% CI 0.34-0.94), whereas adjustment for age and sex showed no significant difference (adjOR 0.70, 95% CI 0.43-1.26). These findings added to the evidence that PWH with access to ART, viral suppression, and a high CD4+ T-cell count did not have a higher risk of severe COVID-19 than people without HIV. The lower unadjusted OR was largely explained by PWH being younger at admission, possibly reflecting a different clinical threshold for admission rather than a true difference in COVID-19 severity. Study II investigated the risk of COVID-19 hospitalisation following confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection in PWH (n=1,348) compared with people without HIV (n=1,669,389) between January 2021 and February 2023. Analyses were categorised by vaccination status and by pandemic period (pre-Omicron, Omicron during public testing, and Omicron after public testing) and ORs were estimated using logistic regression. Among those unvaccinated or vaccinated with only two doses, unadjusted ORs were significantly increased in PWH compared with people without HIV, but not after adjustment for age, sex, month of SARS-CoV-2 infection, income, migrant status, and comorbidities. No significant differences were observed among those vaccinated with at least three doses. During Omicron and public testing, PWH had significantly increased adjusted odds of hospitalisation compared with people without HIV. Taken together, the risk of COVID-19 hospitalisation in PWH was overall similar to that of people without HIV, although temporal variations were observed and may reflect differences in testing patterns by HIV status. Study III estimated the risk ratio (RR) of SARS-CoV-2 vaccination uptake across doses 1-5 using modified Poisson regression, among individuals alive and resident in Sweden at the start of vaccination, comparing PWH (n=7,668) with people without HIV (n=8,168,340). People with HIV were divided into two groups based on COVID-19 vaccination prioritisation: non-vulnerable PWH and clinically vulnerable PWH. In both groups, PWH had lower unadjusted 3-dose uptake (non- vulnerable: RR 0.90, 95% CI 0.88-0.91); however, after adjustment, particularly for country of birth, PWH had higher uptake (non-vulnerable: adjRR 1.17, 95% CI 1.15-1.19). This pattern did not persist among individuals aged ≥65 years, where 5-dose uptake was lower among non-vulnerable PWH (adjRR 0.91, 95% CI 0.85- 0.96). Taken together, PWH had lower vaccination uptake than people without HIV, although it was mainly explained by the higher proportion of PWH born outside Sweden. At the same time, the higher adjusted uptake among PWH underscores that frequent healthcare contact that comes with living with HIV facilitates vaccination, however the same advantage did not appear to extend to older PWH. This further suggests that public health measures addressing vaccination uptake should be directed at migrant populations rather than at PWH. Study IV assessed the risk of severe renal events, cardiovascular events, and all- cause mortality following a T2DM diagnosis in PWH (n=350) compared with people without HIV (n=311,668) between 2010 and 2024. Hazard ratios (HR) were estimated using Cox proportional hazards models adjusted for quintiles of a propensity score based on age, sex, migrant status, comorbidities, and socioeconomic status. A significantly increased risk of major adverse kidney events among PWH compared with people without HIV (adjHR 2.04, 95% CI 1.57- 2.65) was found. No significant differences were observed for major adverse cardiovascular events or all-cause mortality in multivariable models. The renal signal persisted across several sensitivity analyses, however, correction for the known benign serum creatinine increase associated with bictegravir, cobicistat, dolutegravir, and rilpivirine attenuated the estimate and did not remain statistically significant (adjHR 1.32, 95% CI 0.90-1.94). Although detailed exploration of ART was limited by the few events among PWH, these findings highlight the importance of closer monitoring and careful management of renal risk factors in PWH with T2DM and warrant further research to clarify this association. Study I-IV collectively highlight that the previously observed increased comorbidity risk among PWH may be shaped not only by biological vulnerability and ART, but also by social determinants and differences in healthcare access and monitoring. Consequently, the excess comorbidity burden among PWH may be meaningfully reduced by addressing sociodemographic disparities, optimising comorbidity management, and strengthening preventive care, while further research continues to explore the underlying mechanisms and improve risk prediction. List of scientific papers I. Killander Möller I, Gisslen M, Wagner P, Sparen P, Carlander C. COVID-19 hospitalization outcomes in adults by HIV status; a nation-wide register-based study. HIV Med 2023;24(10):1045-55. https://doi.org/10.1111/hiv.13515 II. Killander Möller I, Hedberg P, Wagner P, Lindahl H, Nyström S, Blixt L, et al. Risk of COVID-19 hospitalisation by HIV-status and SARS- CoV-2 vaccination status during pre- and post-Omicron era in a national register-based cohort study in Sweden. 2024;57(2): 178-191. https://doi.org/10.1080/23744235.2024.2405582 III. Killander Möller I, Hedberg P, Wagner P, Sparén P, Gisslén M, Nauclér P, et al. Sociodemographic factors influencing SARS-CoV-2 vaccination uptake in people with and without HIV: Insights from a Swedish Nationwide cohort. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.vaccine.2025.127580 IV. Killander Möller I, Wagner P, Brännström J, Bäckdahl T, Elvstam O, Gisslen M, et al. Renal and Cardiovascular Complications Following Type 2 Diabetes Mellitus in People With and Without HIV: Data from Cohort Study on Morbidity and HIV in Sweden (COSMOHS) between 2010-2024. 2026;82(6):e1252-e1261. https://doi.org/10.1093/cid/ciag261
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed persistent disparities in health outcomes among people with HIV (PWH) despite effective antiretroviral therapy, focusing on the contribution of social determinants and the impact of COVID-19. It aimed to clarify how socioeconomic factors influence the excess risk of age-related comorbidities and COVID-19 outcomes in PWH compared with people without HIV.
Design and population
Four nationwide register-based studies were conducted in Sweden using linked data from virtually the entire adult population or large samples. Study I included 64,815 individuals hospitalised with COVID-19 (121 PWH) from February 2020 to October 2021. Study II compared 1,348 PWH with 1,669,389 people without HIV for COVID-19 hospitalisation from January 2021 to February 2023. Study III assessed COVID-19 vaccination uptake among 7,668 PWH and 8,168,340 people without HIV. Study IV evaluated renal, cardiovascular events, and mortality after type 2 diabetes diagnosis in 350 PWH versus 311,668 people without HIV (2010–2024).
Core methods
Odds ratios (OR) and hazard ratios (HR) were estimated using logistic regression and Cox proportional hazards models, respectively. Analyses adjusted for demographics, socioeconomic factors, comorbidities, and vaccination status. Study II stratified by vaccination status and pandemic period (pre-Omicron, Omicron during public testing, Omicron after public testing). Study III used modified Poisson regression to estimate risk ratios for vaccination uptake.
Main results
In Study I, the unadjusted OR for severe COVID-19 was lower in PWH (OR 0.60, 95% CI 0.34–0.94), but adjustment for age and sex eliminated the difference (adjOR 0.70, 95% CI 0.43–1.26). Study II found increased unadjusted ORs for COVID-19 hospitalisation in unvaccinated or twice-vaccinated PWH, but not after adjustment; during Omicron with public testing, adjusted ORs were significantly higher in PWH. Study III showed lower unadjusted 3-dose vaccination uptake in PWH (RR 0.90, 95% CI 0.88–0.91), but higher adjusted uptake (adjRR 1.17, 95% CI 1.15–1.19), except in those aged ≥65 years (adjRR 0.91, 95% CI 0.85–0.96). Study IV reported a significantly increased risk of major adverse kidney events in PWH with T2DM (adjHR 2.04, 95% CI 1.57–2.65).
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that PWH with access to ART and viral suppression do not have a higher risk of severe COVID-19 than people without HIV, though temporal variations during Omicron highlight potential testing biases. Lower vaccination uptake in PWH appears largely explained by migrant status, while frequent healthcare contact may facilitate vaccination except in older PWH. The increased risk of renal events in PWH with T2DM underscores the need for targeted monitoring.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results indicate that addressing socioeconomic disparities, particularly among migrant populations, may reduce excess comorbidity risks in PWH. Enhanced renal monitoring is warranted in PWH with T2DM. Vaccination programs should prioritise migrant groups to improve uptake.
Limitations and evidence strength
Detailed exploration of antiretroviral therapy effects was limited by few events among PWH, constraining causal inference. The register-based design may introduce residual confounding and misclassification. Despite these limitations, the nationwide scope and adjustment for multiple confounders strengthen the evidence, warranting moderate confidence.

在免对齐 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 谱系分类基准中测量和去除接近重复的污染

Measuring and removing near-duplicate contamination in alignment-free SARS-CoV-2 lineage classification benchmarks
作者： Mohammad Jamhuri, Andy Irawan
摘要中文翻译来自 k 聚体频率分布的无比对谱系分配广泛用于 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 监测,并且执行此操作的方法以一两个百分点的差距相互排名。这些排名基于未经检查的协议。公共存储库保存许多近乎重复的 基因组,并且分层随机分裂将此类组的成员置于分裂的两侧,因此分类器因其已经看到的序列而被记入。这项研究衡量了效果并提供了补救措施。我们提出量化配置文件哈希,它通过对每个频率 媒介 进行舍入并对其进行哈希处理,直接在 k-mer 特征空间中找到接近的重复项。没有序列与任何其他序列进行比较,因此只需传递一次特征矩阵就足够了,无需选择相似性阈值。舍入也是使组得到明确定义的原因,然后它们在训练、验证和测试集中保持完整。在来自 7 个 Pango 谱系的 255,611 个 基因组 上,随机分割使 5.09% 的测试序列在训练中几乎重复,与分隔已发布方法的边缘一样大。然后十个更新规则被训练两次,除了分区之外,其他规则都相同。受污染的基准将一条规则与领先规则分开 0.05;干净的人没有分离。这两个排序不相关,Kendall tau = +0.022,规则平均移动 3.2 个位置,一个基准的领先者在另一个基准上排名第八。因此,在污染下获得的排名并没有说明没有污染的排名,并且除了分数之外值得报告的数量是分裂的泄漏率。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
使用 k-mer 频率分布的无比对谱系分配广泛用于 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 监测,但这些方法的基准排名依赖于尚未针对污染风险进行验证的协议。该研究强调,公共存储库包含许多近乎重复的 基因组,当分层随机分割无意中在训练和测试集中放置重复项时,这可能会夸大明显的分类器性能。
设计与对象
该研究使用跨越七个 Pango 谱系的 255,611 个 基因组 来评估 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 谱系分类基准中近乎重复污染的影响。它比较了污染数据和干净数据分割下的基准排名,除了分区方法之外,以相同的方式训练十个更新规则。
核心方法
作者提出量化配置文件哈希,通过舍入频率 媒介 并对其进行哈希处理,直接在 k-mer 特征空间中检测接近的重复项,确保组在训练、验证和测试集中保持完整。这种方法避免了成对比较,并且无需选择相似性阈值。
主要结果
在随机分割下,5.09% 的测试序列在训练中几乎有重复,这与已发布方法之间的差距相当。受污染的基准测试将一条规则与领先规则的分离度为 0.05,而干净的基准测试则没有分离任何规则。污染和清洁条件下的排名不相关(Kendall tau = +0.022),规则平均移动 3.2 个位置,一个基准的领先者在另一个基准上排名第八。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,基准数据集中的污染可能会对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 谱系分类方法产生误导性的排名。所提出的量化配置文件哈希方法通过消除训练集和测试集之间的泄漏提供了一种实用的补救措施,从而实现更公平的比较。
科研与公卫意义
这项工作强调需要报告泄漏率和基准分数,以确保 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 监测 方法评估的透明度。它为评估谱系分类工具提供了一个可重复的框架,这对于准确的公共卫生 监测 和决策至关重要。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2谱系分类基准,并未评估污染对下游流行病学或临床应用的影响。对于所解决的具体方法问题,证据强度很高,但对其他病毒或环境的普遍性尚未经过测试。

Measuring and removing near-duplicate contamination in alignment-free SARS-CoV-2 lineage classification benchmarks

Authors: Mohammad Jamhuri, Andy Irawan
Original abstract (English)
Alignment-free lineage assignment from k-mer frequency profiles is widely used for SARS-CoV-2 surveillance, and the methods that do it are ranked against each other by margins of one or two percentage points. Those rankings rest on an unchecked protocol. Public repositories hold many near-duplicate genomes, and stratified random splitting puts members of such a group on both sides of the split, so a classifier is credited for sequences it has already seen. This study measures the effect and offers a remedy. We propose quantised profile hashing, which finds near duplicates directly in k-mer feature space by rounding each frequency vector and hashing it. No sequence is compared with any other, so one pass over the feature matrix suffices and no similarity threshold has to be chosen. Rounding is also what makes the groups well defined, and they are then kept whole across the training, validation and test sets. On 255,611 genomes from seven Pango lineages, random splitting leaves 5.09% of test sequences with a near duplicate in training, as large as the margins separating published methods. Ten update rules were then trained twice, identically except for the partition. The contaminated benchmark separates one rule from the leader at 0.05; the clean one separates none. The two orderings are uncorrelated, Kendall tau = +0.022, with rules moving 3.2 positions on average and the leader of one benchmark ranking eighth on the other. A ranking obtained under contamination therefore says nothing about the ranking without it, and the quantity worth reporting beside a score is the leakage rate of the split.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Alignment-free lineage assignment using k-mer frequency profiles is widely used for SARS-CoV-2 surveillance, yet benchmark rankings of these methods rely on protocols that have not been validated for contamination risks. The study highlights that public repositories contain many near-duplicate genomes, which can inflate apparent classifier performance when stratified random splitting inadvertently places duplicates across training and test sets.
Design and population
The study evaluates the impact of near-duplicate contamination in SARS-CoV-2 lineage classification benchmarks using 255,611 genomes spanning seven Pango lineages. It compares benchmark rankings under contaminated and clean data splits, training ten update rules identically except for the partition method.
Core methods
The authors propose quantised profile hashing to detect near duplicates directly in k-mer feature space by rounding frequency vectors and hashing them, ensuring groups are kept intact across training, validation, and test sets. This approach avoids pairwise comparisons and eliminates the need to choose a similarity threshold.
Main results
Under random splitting, 5.09% of test sequences had a near duplicate in training, comparable to the margins separating published methods. The contaminated benchmark separated one rule from the leader at 0.05, while the clean benchmark separated none. Rankings under contamination and clean conditions were uncorrelated (Kendall tau = +0.022), with rules moving an average of 3.2 positions and the leader of one benchmark ranking eighth on the other.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings demonstrate that contamination in benchmark datasets can produce misleading rankings of SARS-CoV-2 lineage classification methods. The proposed quantised profile hashing method provides a practical remedy by eliminating leakage between training and test sets, thereby enabling fairer comparisons.
Scientific and public-health significance
This work underscores the need to report leakage rates alongside benchmark scores to ensure transparency in SARS-CoV-2 surveillance method evaluations. It provides a reproducible framework for assessing lineage classification tools, which is critical for accurate public health surveillance and decision-making.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to SARS-CoV-2 lineage classification benchmarks and does not evaluate the impact of contamination on downstream epidemiological or clinical applications. The evidence strength is high for the specific methodological question addressed, but generalisability to other viruses or contexts remains untested.

Implications for future pandemics from a living systematic review and critical evaluation of observational studies of the effectiveness of COVID-19 vaccination against the Omicron variant.

Implications for future pandemics from a living systematic review and critical evaluation of observational studies of the effectiveness of COVID-19 vaccination against the Omicron variant.
作者： Fatema Kazi, Chin Yang Shapland, Francesca Spiga, Gemma Villanueva, Rosie P Cornish, Nicholas Henschke, Elise Cogo, Meghan Sebastianski, Mohsen Aarabi, Hanna Bergman
摘要中文翻译关于 新型冠状病毒感染 疫苗 在 Omicron 变异占优势期间有效性的可靠证据对于为全球 疫苗接种 策略提供信息至关重要。我们受世界卫生组织 (WHO) 委托进行的活体系统评价旨在对比较观察性研究进行持续综合和严格评估,评估 疫苗 对 Omicron 变体引起的 新型冠状病毒感染/严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的有效性。截至 2022 年 12 月 31 日,每周检索七个数据库,寻找 WHO 批准的 疫苗 平台的证据,包括 mRNA 疫苗(BNT162b2、mRNA-1273)、腺病毒 媒介 疫苗(ChAdOx1-S、SII-ChAdOx1 nCoV-19、 Ad26.COV2·S、Gam-COVID-Vac) 和灭活病毒 疫苗 (CoronaVac、BBV-152)。我们使用干预措施非随机研究中的偏倚风险 (ROBINS-I) 工具来评估 疫苗 有效性估计的有效性。该综述涵盖了超过 5300 万人的 79 项研究,贡献了 2032 项 疫苗 对无症状 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染(36;45.6%)、有症状 新型冠状病毒感染(31;39.2%)、严重或危重 新型冠状病毒感染(53;67.1%)和全因 死亡率 有效性的估计(2;2.5%)。对 疫苗 跨平台、给药方案和人群的性能进行结构化比较,重点关注严重或危重的 新型冠状病毒感染 疾病。据观察,疫苗接种 对 Omicron 变体引起的严重或危重疾病有很强的保护作用,特别是对于 mRNA 疫苗。大多数结果被判断为存在中等偏倚风险(361 项,18%)或存在严重偏倚风险(1591 项,78%),主要问题是混淆。我们讨论对未来大流行防范的影响,包括标准化研究方案的重要性;疫苗接种、测试和结果之间强大的数据链接;并为不同的观察设计验证了偏倚风险框架。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review focuses on the effectiveness of COVID-19 vaccines against the Omicron variant of SARS-CoV-2, particularly in preventing severe or critical disease. The central question is how vaccine platforms perform in reducing the risk of severe outcomes during the Omicron variant's predominance.
证据基础与方法
该审查是世界卫生组织(WHO)委托进行的一项实时系统审查。截至 2022 年 12 月 31 日,每周对七个数据库进行检索,针对 WHO 批准的 疫苗 平台。非随机研究中的偏倚风险 - 干预 (ROBINS-I) 工具用于评估 疫苗 有效性估计的有效性。
共识与结论
The review found that vaccination was strongly protective against severe or critical disease caused by the Omicron variant, with mRNA vaccines showing the highest effectiveness. The analysis included 79 studies involving over 53 million individuals, providing estimates for asymptomatic infection, symptomatic disease, severe/critical disease, and all-cause mortality.
争议与缺口
大多数结果被判断为存在中度或严重偏倚风险,主要是由于混杂因素。关于 疫苗 针对 JN.1 和 BA.2.86 等特定 Omicron 亚变体的有效性的证据有限,需要进一步研究来解决这些差距。
科研与实践启示
该审查强调需要标准化的研究方案、疫苗接种、测试和结果之间强大的数据链接,以及针对不同观察设计的经过验证的偏倚风险框架。这些建议旨在改善未来大流行的防范和 疫苗 评估。

Implications for future pandemics from a living systematic review and critical evaluation of observational studies of the effectiveness of COVID-19 vaccination against the Omicron variant.

Authors: Fatema Kazi, Chin Yang Shapland, Francesca Spiga, Gemma Villanueva, Rosie P Cornish, Nicholas Henschke, Elise Cogo, Meghan Sebastianski, Mohsen Aarabi, Hanna Bergman
Original abstract (English)
Reliable evidence on COVID-19 vaccine effectiveness during the period of Omicron variant predominance was essential to inform global vaccination strategies. Our living systematic review, commissioned by the World Health Organization (WHO), was established to provide an ongoing synthesis and critical appraisal of comparative observational studies evaluating the effectiveness of vaccines against COVID-19/SARS-CoV-2 infection caused by the Omicron variant. Weekly searches covering seven databases up to 31 December 2022 sought evidence for WHO-approved vaccine platforms, including mRNA vaccines (BNT162b2, mRNA-1273), adenoviral vector vaccines (ChAdOx1-S, SII-ChAdOx1 nCoV-19, Ad26.COV2·S, Gam-COVID-Vac) and inactivated virus vaccines (CoronaVac, BBV-152). We used the Risk Of Bias In Non-randomized Studies - of Interventions (ROBINS-I) tool to assess the validity of estimates of vaccine effectiveness. The review included 79 studies of over 53 million individuals, contributing 2032 estimates of vaccine effectiveness against asymptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection (36; 45.6%), symptomatic COVID-19 (31; 39.2%), severe or critical COVID-19 (53; 67.1%) and all-cause mortality (2; 2.5%). Structured comparisons of vaccine performance across platforms, dosing schedules and populations, focussed on severe or critical COVID-19 disease. Vaccination was observed to be strongly protective against severe or critical disease caused by the Omicron variant, particularly for mRNA vaccines. Most results were judged to be at moderate risk of bias (361, 18%) or at serious risk of bias (1591, 78%) with the principal concern being confounding. We discuss implications for future pandemic preparedness, including the importance of standardized study protocols; robust data linkage between vaccination, testing and outcomes; and validated risk-of-bias frameworks for diverse observational designs.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the effectiveness of COVID-19 vaccines against the Omicron variant of SARS-CoV-2, particularly in preventing severe or critical disease. The central question is how vaccine platforms perform in reducing the risk of severe outcomes during the Omicron variant's predominance.
Evidence base and method
The review was conducted as a living systematic review commissioned by the World Health Organization (WHO). Weekly searches were performed across seven databases up to 31 December 2022, targeting WHO-approved vaccine platforms. The Risk Of Bias In Non-randomized Studies - of Interventions (ROBINS-I) tool was used to assess the validity of vaccine effectiveness estimates.
Consensus and conclusions
The review found that vaccination was strongly protective against severe or critical disease caused by the Omicron variant, with mRNA vaccines showing the highest effectiveness. The analysis included 79 studies involving over 53 million individuals, providing estimates for asymptomatic infection, symptomatic disease, severe/critical disease, and all-cause mortality.
Controversies and gaps
Most results were judged to be at moderate or serious risk of bias, primarily due to confounding factors. There is limited evidence on the effectiveness of vaccines against specific Omicron subvariants like JN.1 and BA.2.86, and further research is needed to address these gaps.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for standardized study protocols, robust data linkage between vaccination, testing, and outcomes, and validated risk-of-bias frameworks for diverse observational designs. These recommendations aim to improve future pandemic preparedness and vaccine evaluation.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.
作者： Daniele Focosi, Tommaso Francesco Aiello, Arturo Casadevall, Mario Cruciani, Alessandra D'Abramo, Patrizia De Marco, David Gachoud, Carolina Garcia-Vidal, Claudia Glingani, Thomas Hueso
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review examines the efficacy of high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) in restoring viral clearance among immunocompromised patients with persistent SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following failure of anti-Spike monoclonal antibody (mAb) therapy, focusing on the central question of whether CCP can overcome resistance to mAbs in this population.
证据基础与方法
进行了系统评价和个体参与者数据荟萃分析,检索了 2020 年 1 月至 2026 年 1 月的电子数据库。该方案在 PROSPERO 注册(CRD420251142891)。纳入了 47 例病例(12 项已发表研究中的 30 例和 17 例未发表病例),资格标准包括免疫功能低下患者在 mAb 治疗后 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 持续呈阳性。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,在 70.2% (33/47) 的 mAb 耐药感染免疫功能低下患者中,CCP 输注与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 清除相关,平均输注 2.7 个 CCP 单位。在逻辑回归分析中,CCP 输注总量与病毒清除率呈正相关。
争议与缺口
该综述没有报告长期结果、病毒清除的持久性或与较新的 抗病毒药物 药物的疗效比较。此外,样本量小(47 个案例)和对未发表案例的依赖限制了结论的普遍性和稳健性。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要进行随机对照试验来验证 CCP 在免疫功能低下人群中的疗效并确定最佳剂量策略。建议将 监测 用于对 mAb 和 CCP 均具有抗性的新兴 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体,以指导 治疗 方案。

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.

Authors: Daniele Focosi, Tommaso Francesco Aiello, Arturo Casadevall, Mario Cruciani, Alessandra D'Abramo, Patrizia De Marco, David Gachoud, Carolina Garcia-Vidal, Claudia Glingani, Thomas Hueso
Original abstract (English)
Anti-Spike monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) progressively lost efficacy during the COVID-19 pandemic due to the emergence and predominance of resistant SARS-CoV-2 variants. By contrast, high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) collected from vaccinated donors recently recovered from infection provides a polyclonal source of antibodies that remains effective in clearing SARS-CoV-2 in immunosuppressed patients, unable to mount an adequate immune response against the virus. We conducted a systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis to examine the effect of CCP in immunocompromised patients persistently positive for SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following mAb therapy, and to evaluate possible biological factors associated with a favourable outcome. Electronic databases were searched for studies published from January 2020 to January 2026. All studies including eligible cases were considered in the systematic review. Unpublished cases were also collected from investigators of the selected studies. The protocol was registered at PROSPERO (CRD420251142891). Forty-seven cases (30 cases from 12 published studies and 17 unpublished cases) were included. In 33 out of 47 patients (70.2%) with a mAb-resistant infection, SARS-CoV-2 clearance occurred following CCP transfusion, with a mean of 2.7 CCP units administered. In logistic regression, total CCP volume transfused was positively associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance. In conclusion, CCP transfusion was associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance in persistently positive immunocompromised patients following failure of anti-Spike mAb therapy.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the efficacy of high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) in restoring viral clearance among immunocompromised patients with persistent SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following failure of anti-Spike monoclonal antibody (mAb) therapy, focusing on the central question of whether CCP can overcome resistance to mAbs in this population.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis was conducted, searching electronic databases from January 2020 to January 2026. The protocol was registered at PROSPERO (CRD420251142891). Forty-seven cases (30 from 12 published studies and 17 unpublished cases) were included, with eligibility criteria encompassing immunocompromised patients persistently positive for SARS-CoV-2 after mAb therapy.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that CCP transfusion was associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance in 70.2% (33/47) of immunocompromised patients with mAb-resistant infections, with a mean of 2.7 CCP units administered. Total CCP volume transfused was positively associated with viral clearance in logistic regression analysis.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report long-term outcomes, durability of viral clearance, or comparative efficacy against newer antiviral agents. Additionally, the small sample size (47 cases) and reliance on unpublished cases limit generalizability and robustness of conclusions.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for randomized controlled trials to validate CCP efficacy in immunocompromised populations and to determine optimal dosing strategies. Surveillance for emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants resistant to both mAbs and CCP is recommended to guide treatment protocols.

该综述综合了 2021 年进行的定性访谈(n=25)和 2020 年至 2021 年期间收集的描述性调查数据(n=204),采用社会生态模型指导下的归纳主题分析,探索塑造女性经历的多层次因素。

‘COVID kills, and it's not joking around’: Perceptions and experiences of COVID-19 in Soweto, South Africa

Authors: Sostina S. Matina, Emmanuel Cohen, Kebogile Mokwena, Emily Mendenhall
Original abstract (English)
The COVID-19 pandemic intensified existing social and gender inequalities, disproportionately affecting Black women living in communities like Soweto, South Africa. This paper primarily draws on qualitative data from 25 in-depth interviews conducted in 2021, complemented by descriptive survey data from 204 women collected between 2020 and 2021. We employed inductive thematic analysis guided by the socio-ecological model to examine how intersecting individual, interpersonal, community and societal factors shaped women's experiences during the pandemic. Women played vital caregiving roles, supporting families and communities despite considerable personal stress linked to fears of infection, violence, isolation and economic hardship. On an interpersonal level, stigma related to COVID-19 severely disrupted social bonds, causing mistrust and exclusion within communities. At the societal level, pre-existing distrust in government institutions worsened, contributing to vaccine hesitancy. The widespread looting and unrest observed in Soweto during lockdowns are interpreted as collective resistance reflecting the community's perceived right to survival and fair treatment amid structural neglect. We argue that public health responses must adopt multi-level strategies that recognise women's crucial caregiving roles, address stigma through community engagement, rebuild institutional trust and promote economic justice.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines how the COVID-19 pandemic, caused by SARS-CoV-2, disproportionately impacted Black women in Soweto, South Africa, focusing on intersecting social and gender inequalities and their lived experiences during the pandemic.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes qualitative interviews (n=25) conducted in 2021 and descriptive survey data (n=204) collected between 2020 and 2021, using inductive thematic analysis guided by the socio-ecological model to explore multi-level factors shaping women's experiences.
Consensus and conclusions
Women's caregiving roles were central to family and community support despite significant personal stress, including fears of infection, violence, isolation, and economic hardship. Stigma related to COVID-19 disrupted social bonds, fostering mistrust and exclusion within communities.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report quantitative measures of stigma's impact or the effectiveness of interventions to mitigate it. It also lacks data on long-term mental health outcomes or economic recovery trajectories for affected women.
Research and practice implications
Public health responses should integrate multi-level strategies that acknowledge women's caregiving roles, address stigma through community engagement, rebuild institutional trust, and promote economic justice to address structural inequities exposed during the pandemic.

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.
作者： Agnieszka Kuś, Sebastian Makuch, Jarosław Dybko, Siddarth Agrawal
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review examines the efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) for COVID-19 caused by SARS-CoV-2, focusing on three interacting determinants: timing of administration, antibody titre, and host characteristics, to reconcile conflicting trial outcomes.
证据基础与方法
进行了设计分层叙述综合的系统评价(PRISMA 2020;SWiM),检索 PubMed、Web of Science 和 Scopus,直至 2026 年 5 月,将标准化的 91 场提取矩阵应用于 31 项纳入研究(20,793 名患者;8 项随机对照试验,23 项观察性研究)。
共识与结论
CP 仅在特定条件下有效:早期给药、足够的抗体滴度以及缺乏内源性抗体反应的血清阴性或免疫功能低下的 宿主。在随机对照试验中,6 项中的 4 项发现早期给药有益,4 项中的 3 项发现较高滴度的 CP 有益,两项大型随机对照试验 (n=12,522) 显示对血清阴性受者有优先益处。
争议与缺口
CP 产品中的非 IgG 免疫球蛋白 (IgA/IgM) 的表征几乎不存在,尽管有证据表明它们对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 具有有效的中和活性,但 31 项研究中只有 2 项报告了此类数据,这代表了产品评估中的系统性差距。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先考虑 CP 产品中 IgA/IgM 滴度的标准化量化,并评估未来大流行中被动免疫治疗的精确部署策略,利用时机和滴度决定因素来优化疗效。

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.

Authors: Agnieszka Kuś, Sebastian Makuch, Jarosław Dybko, Siddarth Agrawal
Original abstract (English)
The efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) in COVID-19 has been contested, with large randomized trials producing conflicting results that led to recommendations against routine use. We performed a systematic review with design-stratified narrative synthesis (PRISMA 2020; SWiM; OSF: https://osf.io/tjcuq) to test whether these conflicting outcomes can be reconciled through three interacting determinants-timing, antibody titre, and host characteristics-using a standardized 91-field extraction matrix applied to each study. PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus were searched through May 2026. Thirty-one studies (20,793 patients; 8 RCTs, 23 observational) were included. The synthesis indicates that CP is effective, but only under a specific convergence of conditions: when administered early in the disease course, with sufficient antibody titre, to seronegative or immunocompromised hosts who have not yet mounted an endogenous antibody response. Among RCTs analysing timing, 4 of 6 found benefit with earlier administration; for titre, 3 of 4 RCTs with explicit comparisons found benefit with higher-titre CP; and two large RCTs (combined n = 12,522) demonstrated preferential benefit in seronegative recipients. This interpretation is consistent with modelling estimates that CP, deployed largely to hospitalised patients, nonetheless saved tens of thousands of lives in the United States during the first pandemic year, an effect attributed to the subset treated early enough to modify disease progression. A second, previously unquantified finding concerns antibody class: characterisation of non-IgG immunoglobulins was nearly absent, with only 2 of 31 studies-both from our group-reporting IgA or IgM data, despite evidence that IgA and IgM are among the most potent neutralising classes against SARS-CoV-2. This hypothesis-generating observation identifies a systematic gap in CP product assessment. These findings reframe CP efficacy from a binary question to a conditional one and provide a template for the precision deployment of passive immunotherapy in future pandemics.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) for COVID-19 caused by SARS-CoV-2, focusing on three interacting determinants: timing of administration, antibody titre, and host characteristics, to reconcile conflicting trial outcomes.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review with design-stratified narrative synthesis (PRISMA 2020; SWiM) was conducted, searching PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus through May 2026, applying a standardized 91-field extraction matrix to 31 included studies (20,793 patients; 8 RCTs, 23 observational).
Consensus and conclusions
CP is effective only under specific conditions: early administration, sufficient antibody titre, and in seronegative or immunocompromised hosts lacking endogenous antibody responses. Among RCTs, 4 of 6 found benefit with earlier administration, 3 of 4 with higher-titre CP, and two large RCTs (n=12,522) showed preferential benefit in seronegative recipients.
Controversies and gaps
Characterisation of non-IgG immunoglobulins (IgA/IgM) in CP products is nearly absent, with only 2 of 31 studies reporting such data despite evidence of their potent neutralising activity against SARS-CoV-2, representing a systematic gap in product assessment.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritise standardised quantification of IgA/IgM titres in CP products and evaluate precision deployment strategies for passive immunotherapy in future pandemics, leveraging timing and titre determinants to optimise efficacy.

Psychological interventions to improve maternal mental health and resilience during pregnancy in the COVID-19 pandemic: a systematic review.

Psychological interventions to improve maternal mental health and resilience during pregnancy in the COVID-19 pandemic: a systematic review.
作者： Georgios C. Soultanopoulos, Judith J Idzenga, Astrid M Kamperman, Maria G van Pampus, Johannes C F Ket, Jens Henrichs, Corine J M Verhoeven
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
During the COVID-19 pandemic, pregnant women experienced increased psychological distress, while access to routine mental health care was often disrupted. This systematic review aimed to provide an overview of psychological interventions for pregnant women during the COVID-19 pandemic and to evaluate their effectiveness in reducing distress and enhancing resilience.
证据基础与方法
截至 2025 年 9 月 3 日,对四个数据库进行了系统检索。其中包括评估妊娠期间心理干预的随机和非随机研究。
共识与结论
十六项研究符合纳入标准。大多数研究报告称抑郁、焦虑或压力有所减轻,有些研究发现与复原力相关的因素有所改善。总体而言,适合数字或混合分娩的心理干预措施有望在危机时期改善母亲的情绪健康。
争议与缺口
方法学质量一般;结果测量方法各异且后续行动有限。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先考虑规模更大、方法严谨、结果标准化、随访时间更长的试验。

Psychological interventions to improve maternal mental health and resilience during pregnancy in the COVID-19 pandemic: a systematic review.

Authors: Georgios C. Soultanopoulos, Judith J Idzenga, Astrid M Kamperman, Maria G van Pampus, Johannes C F Ket, Jens Henrichs, Corine J M Verhoeven
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: During the COVID-19 pandemic, pregnant women experienced increased psychological distress, while access to routine mental health care was often disrupted. Psychological interventions may provide an effective strategy to support maternal mental health under these circumstances. OBJECTIVE: This systematic review aimed to provide an overview of psychological interventions for pregnant women during the COVID-19 pandemic and to evaluate their effectiveness in reducing distress and enhancing resilience. METHODS: A systematic search of four databases was conducted up to 3 September 2025. Randomised and non-randomised studies evaluating psychological interventions during pregnancy were included. RESULTS: Sixteen studies met the inclusion criteria. Interventions included cognitive behavioural therapy, mindfulness-based interventions, psychoeducation, and other psychological approaches delivered through individual, group, digital, or hybrid formats. Most studies reported reductions in depression, anxiety, or stress, and some found improvements in resilience-related factors. However, the methodological quality was modest; with heterogeneous outcome measures and limited follow-up. CONCLUSIONS: Overall, psychological interventions adapted for digital or hybrid delivery show promise for improving maternal emotional well-being in times of crises. The findings suggest that accessible and scalable psychological interventions may support maternal mental health during crisis situations when routine care is disrupted. Future research should prioritize larger, methodologically rigorous trials with standardized outcomes, and longer follow-up periods.
View review five-element analysis

During the COVID-19 pandemic, pregnant women experienced increased psychological distress, while access to routine mental health care was often disrupted. Evidence base and method
A systematic search of four databases was conducted up to 3 September 2025. Randomised and non-randomised studies evaluating psychological interventions during pregnancy were included.
Consensus and conclusions
Sixteen studies met the inclusion criteria. Most studies reported reductions in depression, anxiety, or stress, and some found improvements in resilience-related factors. Overall, psychological interventions adapted for digital or hybrid delivery show promise for improving maternal emotional well-being in times of crises.
Controversies and gaps
The methodological quality was modest; with heterogeneous outcome measures and limited follow-up.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize larger, methodologically rigorous trials with standardized outcomes, and longer follow-up periods.

Prevalence of hypertension in severe vs. non-severe COVID-19: a systematic review and meta-analysis.

Prevalence of hypertension in severe vs. non-severe COVID-19: a systematic review and meta-analysis.
作者： Kanwal Khalid, Amr Adel Ahmed Abd El-Aal, Michelle Felicia Lee
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review aimed to investigate the association between hypertension and the severity of coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) by comparing the prevalence of hypertension in severe versus non-severe cases.
证据基础与方法
该审查包括 30 项研究,涉及 15,549 名参与者,使用系统方法来评估这种关联。使用随机效应模型计算合并比值比 (OR)。使用 I2 和 τ2 统计数据评估异质性,并进行亚组分析和元回归以检查地区、样本量、研究质量、年龄和性别的影响。
共识与结论
Hypertension was more frequently observed in severe COVID-19 cases, with a pooled odds ratio (OR) of 2.73 (95% CI: 1.85-4.02, p < 0.00001). However, substantial heterogeneity was noted (I2 = 88%), which limits the precision of the pooled estimate.
争议与缺口
研究特征的异质性限制了证据的一致性和普遍性。亚组分析没有显着解释异质性,并且性别构成与效应大小无关。审查还指出,由于这些限制,应谨慎解释研究结果。
科研与实践启示
The findings may inform clinical risk stratification and public health decision-making regarding hypertension as a risk factor for severe COVID-19.

Prevalence of hypertension in severe vs. non-severe COVID-19: a systematic review and meta-analysis.

Authors: Kanwal Khalid, Amr Adel Ahmed Abd El-Aal, Michelle Felicia Lee
Original abstract (English)
Although numerous studies have investigated the association between hypertension and coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) severity, differences in study characteristics have limited the consistency and generalizability of the available evidence. This systematic review and meta-analysis systematically evaluated the association between hypertension and COVID-19 severity by synthesizing evidence from 30 studies involving 15,549 participants comparing the prevalence of hypertension in non-severe and severe COVID-19 patients. Pooled odds ratios (ORs) were calculated using a random-effects model. Heterogeneity was assessed using I2 and τ2 statistics, while subgroup analyses and meta-regression examined the effects of region, sample size, study quality, age, and gender. Publication bias was evaluated using funnel plots and Egger's test. Hypertension was more frequently observed in severe COVID-19 cases across the included studies with a pooled odds ratio (OR) of 2.73 (95% CI: 1.85-4.02, p < 0.00001), However, substantial heterogeneity was observed (I2 = 88%), limiting the precision of the pooled estimate. Subgroup analysis showed that sample size, NOS scores, and age did not significantly explain heterogeneity, with gender composition also not significantly associated with effect size (β = 0.021, p = 0.38). Funnel plot analysis and Egger's test showed no significant publication bias. Overall, hypertension was associated with an increased likelihood of severe COVID-19, although the high level of heterogeneity suggests that the findings should be interpreted with caution. These findings contribute to the understanding of the association between hypertension and COVID-19 severity and may inform clinical risk stratification and public health decision-making.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review aimed to investigate the association between hypertension and the severity of coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) by comparing the prevalence of hypertension in severe versus non-severe cases.
Evidence base and method
The review included 30 studies with 15,549 participants, using a systematic approach to evaluate the association. Pooled odds ratios (ORs) were calculated using a random-effects model. Heterogeneity was assessed using I2 and τ2 statistics, and subgroup analyses and meta-regression were conducted to examine the effects of region, sample size, study quality, age, and gender.
Consensus and conclusions
Hypertension was more frequently observed in severe COVID-19 cases, with a pooled odds ratio (OR) of 2.73 (95% CI: 1.85-4.02, p < 0.00001). However, substantial heterogeneity was noted (I2 = 88%), which limits the precision of the pooled estimate.
Controversies and gaps
The heterogeneity in study characteristics limited the consistency and generalizability of the evidence. Subgroup analyses did not significantly explain the heterogeneity, and the gender composition was not associated with effect size. The review also noted that the findings should be interpreted with caution due to these limitations.
Research and practice implications
The findings may inform clinical risk stratification and public health decision-making regarding hypertension as a risk factor for severe COVID-19.

Wait times at Christchurch Hospital for adjuvant breast irradiation and the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic.

Wait times at Christchurch Hospital for adjuvant breast irradiation and the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic.
作者： Junya Robinson, Melissa L James, Chris Frampton
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究调查了2018年至2022年基督城医院辅助乳腺放疗的等待时间,重点关注乳腺癌患者从最后一次手术或化疗到第一次放疗的时间间隔。
证据基础与方法
The evidence base consists of a retrospective review of 682 patients who initiated adjuvant radiotherapy at Christchurch Hospital between 1 January 2018 and 31 December 2022. Wait times were defined as the interval in days from final surgery or chemotherapy to the first fraction of radiotherapy and were analyzed for trends, including the impact of COVID-19 lockdown periods.
共识与结论
Median wait time to radiotherapy from surgery was 72 days and from adjuvant chemotherapy was 36 days. A statistically significant annual increase in wait times from surgery to radiotherapy of 7.2 days (95% CI 6.84–7.65 days, p<0.001) was observed. Wait times initially increased but showed a comparative reduction during COVID-19 lockdown periods.
争议与缺口
该研究没有报告观察到的等待时间的增加在复发风险或患者结果方面是否具有临床意义。尚未探讨导致大流行期之后等待时间持续增加的机制。
科研与实践启示
该研究强调需要调查导致辅助乳腺放疗等待时间延长的多因素压力,并采取策略尽量减少延误,特别是降低局部复发的风险。

Wait times at Christchurch Hospital for adjuvant breast irradiation and the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic.

Authors: Junya Robinson, Melissa L James, Chris Frampton
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: This study aimed to quantify wait times for adjuvant breast radiotherapy at Christchurch Hospital following surgery or completion of chemotherapy, with specific attention to the impact of COVID-19 lockdowns on service delivery. METHODS: A retrospective review was conducted of patients with breast cancer who initiated adjuvant radiotherapy between 1 January 2018 and 31 December 2022 at Christchurch Hospital. Wait times were defined as the interval (in days) from final surgery or chemotherapy to the first fraction of radiotherapy. Median wait times were tabulated and graphed to determine trend lines accounting for the COVID-19 lockdown periods affecting Canterbury, New Zealand. RESULTS: The cohort included 682 patients. Median wait time to radiotherapy from surgery was 72 days and from adjuvant chemotherapy was 36 days. A statistically significant increase in wait times was observed from surgery to radiotherapy of 7.2 days per annum (95% confidence interval 6.84-7.65 days, p<0.001). An initial increase in wait times was followed by a comparative reduction during the COVID-19 lockdown period. CONCLUSION: Wait times for adjuvant breast radiotherapy have increased at Christchurch Hospital from 2018 to 2022. Although COVID-19 restrictions temporarily reduced delays, sustained increases suggest multifactorial pressures. Excessive delays to commencing radiation should be minimised to reduce the risk of locoregional recurrence.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study examines wait times for adjuvant breast radiotherapy at Christchurch Hospital from 2018 to 2022, focusing on the interval from final surgery or chemotherapy to the first fraction of radiotherapy in patients with breast cancer.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base consists of a retrospective review of 682 patients who initiated adjuvant radiotherapy at Christchurch Hospital between 1 January 2018 and 31 December 2022. Wait times were defined as the interval in days from final surgery or chemotherapy to the first fraction of radiotherapy and were analyzed for trends, including the impact of COVID-19 lockdown periods.
Consensus and conclusions
Median wait time to radiotherapy from surgery was 72 days and from adjuvant chemotherapy was 36 days. A statistically significant annual increase in wait times from surgery to radiotherapy of 7.2 days (95% CI 6.84–7.65 days, p<0.001) was observed. Wait times initially increased but showed a comparative reduction during COVID-19 lockdown periods.
Controversies and gaps
The study does not report whether the observed increases in wait times are clinically significant in terms of recurrence risk or patient outcomes. The mechanisms driving the sustained increases in wait times beyond the pandemic period are not explored.
Research and practice implications
The study highlights the need to investigate the multifactorial pressures contributing to prolonged wait times for adjuvant breast radiotherapy and to implement strategies to minimize delays, particularly to reduce the risk of locoregional recurrence.

Association of the COVID-19 lockdown and Generalized Anxiety Disorder-7 (GAD-7) in Korean adolescents

Association of the COVID-19 lockdown and Generalized Anxiety Disorder-7 (GAD-7) in Korean adolescents
作者： Do Hyung Kim, Sujin Lee, Jae Ho Chung

范围与问题
The review examines the association between the COVID-19 pandemic and anxiety levels in Korean adolescents, focusing on the prevalence and risk factors for anxiety measured using the Generalized Anxiety Disorder-7 (GAD-7) questionnaire.
证据基础与方法
提供的证据不报告综述类型、数据库、检索日期、资格标准、选择方法或纳入研究计数。它提出了一项使用 2020 年韩国青少年风险行为网络调查的网络自我报告数据进行的单一观察性研究。

Association of the COVID-19 lockdown and Generalized Anxiety Disorder-7 (GAD-7) in Korean adolescents

Authors: Do Hyung Kim, Sujin Lee, Jae Ho Chung
Original abstract (English)
Coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) is a worldwide pandemic that causes anxiety. Research on possible risk factors for anxiety during the COVID-19 pandemic is scarce. In this study, we examined 54,890 participants' web-based self-reported data from the Korea Youth Risk Behavior Web-based Survey in 2020. The Generalized Anxiety Disorder-7 questionnaire was used to measure anxiety levels. The prevalence of anxiety (Generalized Anxiety Disorder-7 score ≥ 10) was 11.2%. Anxiety in adolescents during the COVID-19 pandemic was significantly associated with female gender (odds ratio [OR] = 1.29, 95% confidence interval [CI]: 1.02-1.09), low family affluence scale (OR = 1.17, 95% CI: 1.03-1.34), economic aid (OR = 1.11, 95% CI: 1.01-1.23), financial difficulties caused by COVID-19 (OR = 1.19, 95% CI: 1.11-1.26), substance use (OR = 1.38, 95% CI: 1.05-1.37), asthma (OR = 1.21, 95% CI: 1.08-1.37), allergic rhinitis (OR = 1.08, 95% CI: 1.01-1.15), atopic dermatitis (OR = 1.09, 95% CI: 1.01-1.17), severe stress (OR = 10.01, 95% CI: 8.40-12.03), perceived unhealthy status (OR = 1.67, 95% CI: 1.52-1.82), perceived unhappiness (OR = 2.30, 95% CI: 2.12-3.11), internet addiction (OR = 1.97, 95% CI: 1.85-2.10), school violence experience (OR = 1.59, 95% CI: 1.29-1.97), depression (OR = 2.78, 95% CI: 2.60-2.97), suicidal ideation (OR = 1.94, 95% CI: 1.79-2.11), suicidal plan (OR = 1.48, 95% CI: 1.30-1.69), and suicidal attempt (OR = 1.23, 95% CI: 1.04-1.45). These results highlight the risk factors for anxiety and offer a straightforward method for identifying teenagers who are more likely to experience anxiety during the COVID-19 pandemic.

Scope and question
The review examines the association between the COVID-19 pandemic and anxiety levels in Korean adolescents, focusing on the prevalence and risk factors for anxiety measured using the Generalized Anxiety Disorder-7 (GAD-7) questionnaire.
Evidence base and method
The supplied evidence does not report the review type, databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, selection method, or included-study count. It presents a single observational study using web-based self-reported data from the Korea Youth Risk Behavior Web-based Survey in 2020.
Consensus and conclusions
The prevalence of anxiety (GAD-7 score ≥ 10) among Korean adolescents during the COVID-19 pandemic was 11.2%. Significant risk factors for anxiety included female gender, low family affluence, economic aid, financial difficulties due to COVID-19, substance use, asthma, allergic rhinitis, atopic dermatitis, severe stress, perceived unhealthy status, perceived unhappiness, internet addiction, school violence experience, depression, suicidal ideation, suicidal plan, and suicidal attempt.

Provincializing vulnerability: Rethinking communication inequality in East Asia's COVID-19 research.

Provincializing vulnerability: Rethinking communication inequality in East Asia's COVID-19 research.
作者： Yingjia Huang, Jianfeng Lan
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This article examines how East Asian COVID-19 scholarship conceptualizes vulnerable groups and communication inequality, and what this regional corpus contributes to comparative health communication and the sociology of epidemics.
证据基础与方法
Drawing on bibliometric mapping and article-level thematic coding of 184 peer-reviewed studies published between 2020 and 2025 on China, Japan, and South Korea, the study compares East Asia-focused research with global COVID-19 literature.
共识与结论
研究结果表明,东亚学术界倾向于通过情境系统不匹配来定义脆弱性。当危机治理扰乱儿童、学生、老年人、妇女、残疾人、慢性病患者和低收入群体所依赖的机构、平台、家庭关系和支持系统时,他们就会变得脆弱。
争议与缺口
即使在东亚内部,文献也显示出重要的内部差异。日本研究强调老龄化和常态压力,中国研究强调城乡教育不平等和青年脆弱性,韩国研究在关注数字不平等、性别隔离、歧视和健康管理方面似乎更接近全球研究。
科研与实践启示
它认为,未来的大流行防范需要包容性的通信系统以及医疗和流行病学能力。

Provincializing vulnerability: Rethinking communication inequality in East Asia's COVID-19 research.

Authors: Yingjia Huang, Jianfeng Lan
Original abstract (English)
This article examines how East Asian COVID-19 scholarship conceptualizes vulnerable groups and communication inequality, and what this regional corpus contributes to comparative health communication and the sociology of epidemics. Drawing on bibliometric mapping and article-level thematic coding of 184 peer-reviewed studies published between 2020 and 2025 on China, Japan, and South Korea, the study compares East Asia-focused research with global COVID-19 literature. The findings show that East Asian scholarship tends to define vulnerability through situational system mismatch. Children, students, older adults, women, people with disabilities, chronic disease patients, and low-income groups become vulnerable when crisis governance disrupts the institutions, platforms, family relations, and support systems on which they depend. The article further identifies four mechanisms through which communication inequality intensifies vulnerability: institutional and technological exclusion, failures of understanding and judgment, interruptions of support, and governance exposure and stigma amplification. Even within East Asia, the literature shows important internal differences. Japanese studies foreground aging and norm pressure, Chinese studies emphasize urban-rural educational inequality and youth vulnerability, and Korean studies appear closer to global research in their attention to digital inequality, gendered isolation, discrimination, and health management. By bringing East Asian scholarship into comparative pandemic research, this article shows how vulnerability can be generated through everyday system configurations and crisis communication infrastructures. It argues that future pandemic preparedness requires inclusive communication systems alongside medical and epidemiological capacity.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This article examines how East Asian COVID-19 scholarship conceptualizes vulnerable groups and communication inequality, and what this regional corpus contributes to comparative health communication and the sociology of epidemics.
Evidence base and method
Drawing on bibliometric mapping and article-level thematic coding of 184 peer-reviewed studies published between 2020 and 2025 on China, Japan, and South Korea, the study compares East Asia-focused research with global COVID-19 literature.
Consensus and conclusions
The findings show that East Asian scholarship tends to define vulnerability through situational system mismatch. Children, students, older adults, women, people with disabilities, chronic disease patients, and low-income groups become vulnerable when crisis governance disrupts the institutions, platforms, family relations, and support systems on which they depend.
Controversies and gaps
Even within East Asia, the literature shows important internal differences. Japanese studies foreground aging and norm pressure, Chinese studies emphasize urban-rural educational inequality and youth vulnerability, and Korean studies appear closer to global research in their attention to digital inequality, gendered isolation, discrimination, and health management.
Research and practice implications
It argues that future pandemic preparedness requires inclusive communication systems alongside medical and epidemiological capacity.

Impact of COVID-19 Pandemic Restrictions on Maxillofacial and Oral Surgery Services at Chris Hani Baragwanath Academic Hospital.

Impact of COVID-19 Pandemic Restrictions on Maxillofacial and Oral Surgery Services at Chris Hani Baragwanath Academic Hospital.
作者： Lebogang E Mbengo, R. E. Rikhotso
摘要中文翻译背景 新型冠状病毒感染 大流行导致医疗保健服务仅限于主要针对紧急病例,并广泛削减常规临床服务。目的 评估 新型冠状病毒感染 大流行限制对颌面和口腔外科 (MFOS) 服务的影响。方法 我们对 2020 年 3 月 26 日至 12 月 28 日(新型冠状病毒感染 期间)管理的 MFOS 患者进行了回顾性记录审查研究,并将这些患者与 1 年前的新冠肺炎爆发前的相同时期进行了比较。收集的数据包括患者诊断、治疗 类型和 治疗 设置等变量。使用描述性统计总结诊断和 治疗 程序的分布,并使用折线图说明趋势。2个时期之间的比较采用χ2检验,P值<0.05被认为有统计学意义。结果 新型冠状病毒感染 期间共咨询了 2926 名患者,下降了 41.88%(2019 年为 5032 例,2020 年为 2926 例)(P<0.0001)。全身麻醉手术从2019年的453例减少到2020年的272例,减少了39.96%(P=0.000448)。创伤相关诊断从 2019 年的 249 例减少到 2020 年的 154 例(P=0.556)。在警报级别 4 和 5 中观察到最大的下降。创伤是 诊断 期间最常见的 诊断,其次是脓毒症和病理。19 岁至 35 岁年龄组的病例数在所有时期都是最高的。结论 新型冠状病毒感染 大流行和国家封锁的实施导致 MFOS 服务大幅下降。我们的研究结果表明,规范酒精销售和分销可以对减少创伤入院产生积极影响。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review evaluates the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic restrictions on maxillofacial and oral surgery (MFOS) services at Chris Hani Baragwanath Academic Hospital. The central question is how these restrictions affected the volume and types of surgical procedures and diagnoses during the pandemic period compared to the pre-pandemic period.
证据基础与方法
The review is a retrospective, record review study comparing MFOS patients managed during the COVID-19 period (26 March to 28 December 2020) with the same pre-COVID period one year earlier. Descriptive statistics and χ2 tests were used to summarize and compare the distribution of diagnoses and treatment procedures, with a P-value of <0.05 considered statistically significant.
共识与结论
研究发现,疫情期间MFOS服务大幅下降,患者咨询量减少了41.88%(从2019年的5032人次减少到2020年的2926人次,P<0.0001)。全身麻醉手术也减少了 39.96%(从 453 例减少到 272 例,P=0.000448)。与创伤相关的诊断从 249 例减少到 154 例(P=0.556),但创伤仍然是大流行期间最常见的 诊断。服务下降幅度最大的是警报级别 4 和 5。
争议与缺口
该综述没有提供有关创伤相关诊断下降背后的具体机制或原因的详细信息,也没有探讨这些变化对患者结果或医疗保健系统的更广泛影响。该研究侧重于描述性统计,并未解决潜在的混杂因素或长期影响。
科研与实践启示
调查结果表明,大流行限制严重影响了 MFOS 服务,特别是在高度戒备状态下。该审查表明,规范酒精销售和分销可能有助于减少创伤入院率,强调需要采取有针对性的公共卫生干预措施,以减轻大流行对外科服务的间接影响。

Impact of COVID-19 Pandemic Restrictions on Maxillofacial and Oral Surgery Services at Chris Hani Baragwanath Academic Hospital.

Authors: Lebogang E Mbengo, R. E. Rikhotso
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND The COVID-19 pandemic resulted in a limitation of health care services to mainly emergency cases and widespread curtailment of routine clinical services. OBJECTIVE To evaluate the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic restrictions on maxillofacial and oral surgery (MFOS) services. METHODS We conducted a retrospective, record review study of MFOS patients managed between 26 March and 28 December 2020 (COVID-19 period) and compared these with the same pre-COVID period 1 year previously. Data collected included variables such as patient diagnoses, type of treatment, and treatment setting. Descriptive statistics were used to summarize the distribution of diagnoses and treatment procedures, and trends were illustrated using line graphs. Comparisons between the 2 periods were performed using χ2 tests, with a P-value of <0.05 considered statistically significant. RESULTS A total of 2926 patients were consulted during the COVID-19 period, constituting a 41.88% decline (5032 in 2019 versus 2926 in 2020) (P<0.0001). General anesthetic procedures decreased from 453 cases in 2019 to 272 in 2020, a 39.96% reduction (P=0.000448). Trauma-related diagnoses decreased from 249 cases in 2019 to 154 cases in 2020 (P=0.556). The largest drop was observed in alert levels 4 and 5. Trauma was the most frequent diagnosis in the COVID-19 period, followed by sepsis and pathology. The 19- to 35-year age group recorded the highest number of cases across all periods. CONCLUSION The COVID-19 pandemic and the implementation of the national lockdown resulted in a substantial decline in MFOS services. Our findings suggest that regulating alcohol sales and distribution can have a positive impact on reducing trauma admissions.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review evaluates the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic restrictions on maxillofacial and oral surgery (MFOS) services at Chris Hani Baragwanath Academic Hospital. The central question is how these restrictions affected the volume and types of surgical procedures and diagnoses during the pandemic period compared to the pre-pandemic period.
Evidence base and method
The review is a retrospective, record review study comparing MFOS patients managed during the COVID-19 period (26 March to 28 December 2020) with the same pre-COVID period one year earlier. Descriptive statistics and χ2 tests were used to summarize and compare the distribution of diagnoses and treatment procedures, with a P-value of <0.05 considered statistically significant.
Consensus and conclusions
The study found a substantial decline in MFOS services during the pandemic, with a 41.88% reduction in patient consultations (from 5032 in 2019 to 2926 in 2020, P<0.0001). General anesthetic procedures also decreased by 39.96% (from 453 to 272 cases, P=0.000448). Trauma-related diagnoses decreased from 249 to 154 cases (P=0.556), but trauma remained the most frequent diagnosis during the pandemic. The largest drop in services was observed in alert levels 4 and 5.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not provide detailed information on the specific mechanisms or reasons behind the decline in trauma-related diagnoses, nor does it explore the broader implications of these changes on patient outcomes or healthcare systems. The study focuses on descriptive statistics and does not address potential confounding factors or long-term effects.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest that pandemic restrictions significantly impacted MFOS services, particularly in high alert levels. The review implies that regulating alcohol sales and distribution could help reduce trauma admissions, highlighting the need for targeted public health interventions to mitigate the indirect effects of the pandemic on surgical services.

Comparative analysis of associations between facility structure and air quality managers' knowledge, attitudes, and practices by COVID-19 outbreak status in welfare facilities for persons with disabilities: a cross-sectional survey study in South Korea.

Comparative analysis of associations between facility structure and air quality managers' knowledge, attitudes, and practices by COVID-19 outbreak status in welfare facilities for persons with disabilities: a cross-sectional survey study in South Korea.
作者： Micheal S Yu, B. I. Kim, Ju Yu, Sanghwan Bae, Seyoung Kim, Kang SJ, Sang-Eun Lee
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This study investigated associations between building structure and managers' knowledge, attitudes, and practices (KAP) regarding indoor air quality in welfare facilities for persons with disabilities, comparing facilities with and without coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) cluster outbreaks.
证据基础与方法
对大韩民国 242 个残疾人福利设施的管理人员进行了一项网络调查。设施是从约 3,000 个设施的全国抽样框架中招募的,响应率为 8.1%。这份包含 80 项的调查问卷评估了建筑特征和与空气质量相关的 KAP 分数。使用逻辑回归来识别与聚类 暴发 相关的因素。
共识与结论
Building structure, particularly complex/multi-use configuration, was associated with COVID-19 cluster outbreaks. Facilities in complex/multi-use buildings had higher odds of outbreaks than those in detached buildings did (adjusted odds ratio, 2.21; 95% confidence interval, 1.25-3.91; p=0.006).
争议与缺口
对于管理因素,具有 暴发 的机构往往态度得分较低 (p=0.02);然而,在 Bonferroni 校正后(调整后的 α=0.0083),这种关联并没有保持统计显着性。知识或实践分数没有观察到显着差异。
科研与实践启示
在保守显着性阈值下,管理 KAP 分数与 暴发 没有明确的相关性,但观察到的趋势表明,机构支持、通风指导和设施管理人员的实践培训可能有助于加强未来的感染预防工作。

Comparative analysis of associations between facility structure and air quality managers' knowledge, attitudes, and practices by COVID-19 outbreak status in welfare facilities for persons with disabilities: a cross-sectional survey study in South Korea.

Authors: Micheal S Yu, B. I. Kim, Ju Yu, Sanghwan Bae, Seyoung Kim, Kang SJ, Sang-Eun Lee
Original abstract (English)
Objectives This study investigated associations between building structure and managers' knowledge, attitudes, and practices (KAP) regarding indoor air quality in welfare facilities for persons with disabilities, comparing facilities with and without coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) cluster outbreaks. Methods A web-based survey was conducted among managers of 242 welfare facilities for persons with disabilities in the Republic of Korea. Facilities were recruited from a national sampling frame of approximately 3,000 facilities, yielding a response rate of 8.1%. The 80-item questionnaire assessed building characteristics and air quality-related KAP scores. Logistic regression was used to identify factors associated with cluster outbreaks. Results Of the 242 facilities, 50.8% had experienced cluster outbreaks. Facilities in complex/multi-use buildings had higher odds of outbreaks than those in detached buildings did (adjusted odds ratio, 2.21; 95% confidence interval, 1.25-3.91; p=0.006). For managerial factors, facilities with outbreaks tended to have lower attitude scores (p=0.02); however, this association did not remain statistically significant after Bonferroni correction (adjusted α=0.0083). No significant differences were observed in knowledge or practice scores. Conclusion Building structure, particularly complex/multi-use configuration, was associated with COVID-19 cluster outbreaks. Managerial KAP scores were not definitively associated with outbreaks under the conservative significance threshold, but the observed trends suggest that institutional support, ventilation guidance, and practical training for facility managers may help strengthen future infection-prevention efforts.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study investigated associations between building structure and managers' knowledge, attitudes, and practices (KAP) regarding indoor air quality in welfare facilities for persons with disabilities, comparing facilities with and without coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) cluster outbreaks.

Controversies and gaps

Concentration of hospital utilization in the capital area among elderly inpatients from non-capital regions in South Korea: Before and during the COVID-19 pandemic.

Concentration of hospital utilization in the capital area among elderly inpatients from non-capital regions in South Korea: Before and during the COVID-19 pandemic.
作者： Yeaeun Kim, Jihoon Lee, Jongho Park
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The study aimed to analyze the sustained concentration of hospital utilization in the capital area among elderly inpatients from non-capital regions in South Korea, both before and during the COVID-19 pandemic, and to identify the characteristics of those who utilized these facilities during the pandemic.
证据基础与方法
Data were obtained from the Korea National Hospital Discharge In-Depth Injury Survey (KNHDIS) for the periods 2018-2019 (pre-COVID-19) and 2020-2021 (during COVID-19). Statistical analyses, including chi-square tests and logistic regression, were conducted using SAS 9.4 to examine changes in capital-area hospitalization and to identify the characteristics of the study subjects during the pandemic.
共识与结论
The overall rate of capital-area hospitalization among regional elderly patients remained stable at 7.5% both before and during the COVID-19 pandemic. Detailed results revealed statistically significant shifts across specific age groups and main diagnoses, with increases observed in some characteristics.
争议与缺口
该研究没有报告观察到的与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 相关的住院模式变化的具体机制或影响。它还没有解决这些模式对病毒传播或区域医疗系统有效性的潜在影响。
科研与实践启示
首都地区住院治疗持续集中,需要紧急实施旨在振兴地区医疗系统的政府政策。鼓励非首都地区的医院根据使用首都地区医院的老年地区患者的特点,对设施和先进医疗设备进行战略性投资,并制定有针对性的策略来吸引地区患者。

Concentration of hospital utilization in the capital area among elderly inpatients from non-capital regions in South Korea: Before and during the COVID-19 pandemic.

Authors: Yeaeun Kim, Jihoon Lee, Jongho Park
Original abstract (English)
Hospital utilization in South Korea exhibits a significant concentration in the capital area, posing a persistent social issue. Given the heightened vulnerability of elderly patients to COVID-19, their continued travel to capital-area hospitals during the pandemic could have facilitated rapid virus dissemination, necessitating a review of the regional healthcare delivery system. This study therefore aimed to analyze the sustained concentration of hospital utilization in the capital area among regional elderly patients and to identify the characteristics of those who utilized these facilities during the pandemic. Data for this study were obtained from the Korea National Hospital Discharge In-Depth Injury Survey (KNHDIS) for the periods 2018-2019 (pre-COVID-19) and 2020-2021 (during COVID-19). A total of 6,026,155 weighted patient data points for non-capital area residents aged over 65 years were analyzed. Statistical analyses, including chi-square tests and logistic regression, were conducted using SAS 9.4 to examine changes in capital-area hospitalization and to identify the characteristics of the study subjects during the pandemic. The overall rate of capital-area hospitalization among regional elderly patients remained stable at 7.5% both before and during the COVID-19 pandemic. However, detailed results revealed statistically significant shifts across specific age groups and main diagnoses, with increases observed in some characteristics. Logistic regression analysis indicated that during the pandemic, regional elderly patients with diagnoses of cancer, higher disease severity (CCI > 3 points), and those undergoing surgery demonstrated a significantly higher likelihood of hospitalizing in the capital area compared to their respective comparison groups. The observed persistent concentration of hospitalizations in the capital area necessitates the urgent implementation of governmental policies aimed at revitalizing regional medical systems. Furthermore, hospitals located in non-capital areas are urged to strategically invest in facilities and advanced medical devices and develop targeted strategies to attract regional patients, considering the identified characteristics of elderly regional patients who utilize capital-area hospitals.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study aimed to analyze the sustained concentration of hospital utilization in the capital area among elderly inpatients from non-capital regions in South Korea, both before and during the COVID-19 pandemic, and to identify the characteristics of those who utilized these facilities during the pandemic.
Evidence base and method
Data were obtained from the Korea National Hospital Discharge In-Depth Injury Survey (KNHDIS) for the periods 2018-2019 (pre-COVID-19) and 2020-2021 (during COVID-19). Statistical analyses, including chi-square tests and logistic regression, were conducted using SAS 9.4 to examine changes in capital-area hospitalization and to identify the characteristics of the study subjects during the pandemic.
Consensus and conclusions
The overall rate of capital-area hospitalization among regional elderly patients remained stable at 7.5% both before and during the COVID-19 pandemic. Detailed results revealed statistically significant shifts across specific age groups and main diagnoses, with increases observed in some characteristics.
Controversies and gaps
The study does not report on the specific mechanisms or implications of the observed shifts in hospitalization patterns related to SARS-CoV-2. It also does not address the potential impact of these patterns on the spread of the virus or the effectiveness of regional healthcare systems.
Research and practice implications
The observed persistent concentration of hospitalizations in the capital area necessitates the urgent implementation of governmental policies aimed at revitalizing regional medical systems. Hospitals located in non-capital areas are urged to strategically invest in facilities and advanced medical devices and develop targeted strategies to attract regional patients, considering the identified characteristics of elderly regional patients who utilize capital-area hospitals.
